Coaches the House footer and cricket, and takes an intelligent interest in things generally. Then there are some decent fellows in Merevale’s. Charteris, Welch, Graham, Thomson, heaps of them.”


“Pity you came to Ward’s,” said the Babe. “Why did you?”


===Chinese===

“巴不得‘共同’走人，”沃恩说，“只可惜那种家伙总是赖着不死不滚，直到咽气或被开革。”


“我看他不像是会被开革的那种人，”宝贝说。


“恐怕不是。但愿我能调去别的社。有沃德和‘共同’两头夹击，这日子没法过。”


“就说梅雷维尔社吧，”沃恩说，“真希望我在那儿。头一条，有梅雷维尔本人。在老师里，他算得离完人最近的了。指导本社足球和板球，对万事万物都有一副聪明的热心肠。再者，梅雷维尔社有些像样的人。查特里斯、韦尔奇、格雷厄姆、汤姆森，一抓一大把。”


“可惜你进了沃德社，”宝贝说，“当初怎么来的？”


===Original===

“My pater knew Ward a bit. If he’d known him well, he’d have sent me somewhere else.”


“My pater knew Vaughan’s pater well, who knew Ward slightly and there you are. *Voilà comme des accidents arrivent.*”


“If Ward wanted to lug in a day boy to be head of the House,” said Vaughan, harping once more on the old string, “he might at least have got somebody decent.”


“There’s the great Babe himself. Babe, why don’t you come in next term?”


“Not much,” said the Babe, with a shudder.


===Chinese===

“我老爸跟沃德有一面之缘。他要是深知其人，早把我送别处去了。”


“我老爸跟沃恩他老爸交情甚笃，后者跟沃德有一面之缘——于是乎。*Voilà comme des accidents arrivent.*（瞧，祸事就这么巧起来了。）”


“沃德既然要拖个走读生来当社长，”沃恩又弹起老调，“总也该拖个体面点儿的来。”


“眼前不就有伟大的宝贝本人嘛。宝贝，下学期你怎么不搬进来？”


“才不呢，”宝贝说着，打了个寒噤。


===Original===

“Well, even barring present company, there are lots of chaps who would have jumped at the chance of being head of a House. But nothing would satisfy Ward but lugging the Mutual from the bosom of his beastly family.”


“We haven’t decided that point about where he goes to,” said the Babe.


At this moment the door of the study opened, and the gentleman in question appeared in person. He stood in the doorway for a few seconds, gasping and throwing his arms about as if he found a difficulty in making his way in.


“I wish you two wouldn’t make such an awful froust in the study *every* afternoon,” he observed, pleasantly. “Have you been having a little tea party? How nice!”


“We’ve been brewing, if that’s what you mean,” said Vaughan, shortly.


===Chinese===

“好嘛，就算在场诸位不算，想当社长、一有机会就扑上去的人也海了去了。可沃德谁都不中意，非把‘共同’从他那讨厌家庭的怀抱里拖出来不可。”


“可他到底去哪儿，我们还没讨论出结果呢，”宝贝说。


正此时，书房门开了，谈论中的那位绅士亲身登场。他在门口立了几秒钟，又是喘气又是挥舞双臂，仿佛进门路上困难重重。


“我希望你们二位别*每*天下午都在书房里炮制这等可怕的浊气，”他和颜悦色地说，“你们开了个小茶会？真好！”


“要是你指的是煮茶，那没错，”沃恩简慢地说。


===Original===

“Oh,” said Plunkett, “I hope you enjoyed yourselves. It’s nearly lockup, MacArthur.”


“That’s Plunkett’s delicate way of telling you you’re not wanted, Babe.”


“Well, I suppose I ought to be going,” said the Babe. “So long.”


And he went, feeling grateful to Providence for not having made his father, like the fathers of Vaughan and Dallas, a casual acquaintance of Mr. Ward.


The Mutual Friend really was a trial to Vaughan and Dallas. Only those whose fate it is or has been to share a study with an uncongenial companion can appreciate their feelings to the full. Three in a study is always something of a tight fit, and when the three are in a state of perpetual warfare, or, at the best, of armed truce, things become very bad indeed.


===Chinese===

“哦，”普伦基特说，“希望你们尽兴了。快锁门了，麦克阿瑟。”


“这就是普伦基特委婉地下逐客令呢，宝贝。”


“好吧，我该走了，”宝贝说，“回见。”


于是他走了，一路感念上苍：幸而没有让他老子像沃恩和达拉斯的老子一样，跟沃德先生结下泛泛之交。


“共同的朋友”对沃恩和达拉斯而言实在是一场磨难。唯有命里注定（或曾经注定）要与一个格格不入的同伴合用书房的人，才能充分体会他们的心情。三个人挤一间书房本就局促，而一旦这三方处于永久战争状态，或至多武装休战，事情就实在糟透了。


===Original===

“Do you find it necessary to have tea parties every evening?” enquired Plunkett, after he had collected his books for the night’s work. “The smell of burnt meat﻿—”


“Fried sausages,” said Vaughan. “Perfectly healthy smell. Do you good.”


“It’s quite disgusting. Really, the air in here is hardly fit to breathe.”


“You’ll find an excellent brand of air down in the senior study,” said Dallas, pointedly. “Don’t stay and poison yourself here on *our* account,” he added. “Think of your family.”


“I shall work where I choose,” said the Mutual Friend, with dignity.


===Chinese===

“你们认为每晚开茶会是必要的吗？”普伦基特收拢了夜里用功要用的书之后，质问道。“这股焦肉味——”


“煎香肠，”沃恩说，“再健康不过的气味了。对你有好处。”


“恶心透了。说真的，这里的空气简直不宜呼吸。”


“高年级书房里有一种上等品牌的空气，”达拉斯话里有话地说，“别为了*我们*委屈自己留在这儿中毒，”他补了一句，“替你家人想想。”


“我乐意在哪儿用功就在哪儿用功，”“共同的朋友”庄严地说。


===Original===

“Of course, so long as you do work. You mustn’t talk. Vaughan and I have got some Livy to do.”


Plunkett snorted, and the passage of arms ended, as it usually did, in his retiring with his books to the senior study, leaving Dallas and Vaughan to discuss his character once more in case there might be any points of it left upon which they had not touched in previous conversations.


“This robbery of the pots is a rum thing,” said Vaughan, thoughtfully, when the last shreds of Plunkett’s character had been put through the mincing machine to the satisfaction of all concerned.


“Yes. It’s the sort of thing one doesn’t think possible till it actually happens.”


“What the dickens made them put the things in the Pav. at all? They must have known it wouldn’t be safe.”


===Chinese===

“当然，只要你肯用功就行。但你别说话。我和沃恩还有点李维（Livy）要对付。”


普伦基特哼了一声。这场交锋一如既往地以他抱着书撤到高年级书房告终，留下达拉斯和沃恩再次研讨他的人品，唯恐从前多次谈话中还有哪一点没数落到的。


“偷奖杯这案子真是蹊跷，”沃恩若有所思地说。此时普伦基特人格的最后残片已在绞肉机里绞了个皆大欢喜。


“是啊。这种事，不真发生，谁也想不到可能。”


“到底见什么鬼要把东西搁休息亭？他们总该如何道那儿不保险。”


===Original===

“Well, you see, they usually cart them into the board room, I believe, only this time the governors were going to have a meeting there. They couldn’t very well meet in a room with the table all covered with silver pots.”


“Don’t see why.”


“Well, I suppose they could, really, but some of the governors are fairly nuts on strict form. There’s that crock who makes the two-hour vote of thanks speeches on Prize Day. You can see him rising to a point of order, and fixing the Old ’Un with a fishy eye.”


“Well, anyhow, I don’t see that they can blame a burglar for taking the pots if they simply chuck them in his way like that.”


“No. I say, we’d better weigh in with the Livy. The man Ward’ll be round directly. Where’s the dic.? *And* our invaluable friend, Mr. Bohn? Right. Now, you reel it off, and I’ll keep an eye on the notes.” And they settled down to the business of the day.


===Chinese===

“呃，是这样的，我相信他们通常都把奖品运进会议室，只是这回校董们要在那儿开会。让他们在一张堆满银杯的桌子上开会，总不太像话。”


“我看不出有什么不像话。”


“呃，真要开倒也开得成，只是有些校董对刻板礼数痴迷得很。就发奖日上发表两小时答谢演说的那老古董，你都能想见他会站起来提程序问题，拿一双死鱼眼把老头子钉在原地。”


“总之，他们把银杯这么随手往贼的必经之路上扔，我就看不出有什么脸面怪贼拿。”


“说的是。我说，咱们还是赶紧对付李维吧。沃德那家伙随时会过来。词典（dic.）呢？还有咱们那位无价的朋友博恩先生（Mr. Bohn）？好。你背原文，我盯注释。”于是他们安顿下来，处理当天的正业。


===Original===

After a while Vaughan looked up.


“Who’s going to win the mile?” he asked.


“What’s the matter with Thomson?”


“How about Drake then?”


“Thomson won the half.”


===Chinese===

过了一阵，沃恩抬起头来。


“一英里谁会赢？”他问。


“汤姆森有什么问题吗？”


“那德雷克呢？”


“半英里是汤姆森赢的。”


===Original===

“I knew you’d say that. The half isn’t a test of a chap’s mile form. Besides, did you happen to see Drake’s sprint?”


“Jolly good one.”


“I know, but look how late he started for it. Thomson crammed on the pace directly he got into the straight. Drake only began to put it on when he got to the Pav. Even then he wasn’t far behind at the tape.”


“No. Well, I’m not plunging either way. Ought to be a good race.”


“Rather. I say, I wonder Welch doesn’t try his hand at the mile. I believe he would do some rattling times if he’d only try.”


===Chinese===

“我就知道你会这么说。半英里检验不了一个人的英里状态。再说，你碰巧看见德雷克的冲刺了吗？”


“相当精彩。”


“我知道，可你瞧他冲刺启动得多晚。汤姆森一进直道就猛加了速度。德雷克到了休息亭跟前才发力。饶是如此，撞线时他也没落后多少。”


“是啊。反正我两边都不押重注。想必是场好比赛。”


“必是。我说，韦尔奇为什么不试试一英里呢，真想不透。我相信他只要肯试，能跑出呱呱叫的成绩。”


===Original===

“Why, Welch is a sprinter.”


“I know. But I believe for all that that the mile’s his distance. He’s always well up in the cross-country runs.”


“Anyhow, he’s not in for it this year. Thomson’s my man. It’ll be a near thing, though.”


“Jolly near thing. With Drake in front.”


“Thomson.”


===Chinese===

“嗐，韦尔奇是短跑的。”


“我知道。可我仍相信一英里才是他的距离。越野跑他向来名列前茅。”


“反正他今年没报名。我押汤姆森。不过会是一场险胜。”


“险得很呢。德雷克领先。”


“汤姆森。”


===Original===

“Drake.”


“All right, we’ll see. Wonder why the beak doesn’t come up. I can’t sit here doing Livy all the evening. And yet if we stop he’s bound to look in.”


“Oh Lord, is that what you’ve been worrying about? I thought you’d developed the work habit or something. Ward’s all right. He’s out on the tiles tonight. Gone to a dinner at Philpott’s.”


“Good man, how do you know? Are you certain?”


“Heard him telling Prater this morning. Half the staff have gone. Good opportunity for a chap to go for a stroll if he wanted to. Shall we, by the way?”


===Chinese===

“德雷克。”


“行，走着瞧。怪了，学监怎么还不来。我可没法整晚坐在这儿啃李维。可我们一停，他保准就来探头。”


“我的老天，你操的就是这份心？我还当你养成了用功的习惯什么的。沃德没事。他今晚出去撒欢了。赴菲尔波特家的饭局去了。”


“好小子，你怎么知道的？确定吗？”


“今早听见他跟普拉特（Prater）说的。教职员工走了一半。谁要想出去遛个弯，正是良机。话说，咱们去不去？”


===Original===

“Not for me, thanks. I’m in the middle of a rather special book. Ever read *Great Expectations*? Dickens, you know.”


“I know. Haven’t read it, though. Always rather funk starting on a classic, somehow. Good?”


“My dear chap! Good’s not the word.”


“Well, after you. Exit Livy, then. And a good job, too. You might pass us the great Sherlock. Thanks.”


He plunged with the great detective into the mystery of the speckled band, while Vaughan opened *Great Expectations* at the place where he had left off the night before. And a silence fell upon the study.


===Chinese===

“我就算了，谢了。我正读到一本相当特别的书的半截。读过《远大前程》（*Great Expectations*）吗？狄更斯（Dickens）的，你知道。”


“我知道。不过没读过。不知怎么，一到要开读经典就发怵。好吗？”


“亲爱的伙计！‘好’这个字都嫌不够。”


“那好，你先请。李维退场。也是桩好事。麻烦把大侦探夏洛克递给我。谢了。”


他随大侦探一头扎进《斑点带子案》（speckled band）的谜团，沃恩则把《远大前程》翻到昨晚搁下的地方。书房里一片沉静。


===Original===

Curiously enough, Dallas was not the only member of Ward’s House to whom it occurred that evening that the absence of the Housemaster supplied a good opportunity for a stroll. The idea had also struck Plunkett favourably. He was not feeling very comfortable downstairs. On entering the senior study he found Galloway, an Upper Fourth member of the House, already in possession. Galloway had managed that evening to insinuate himself with such success into the good graces of the matron, that he had been allowed to stay in the House instead of proceeding with the rest of the study to the Great Hall for preparation. The palpable failure of his attempt to hide the book he was reading under the table when he was disturbed led him to cast at the Mutual Friend, the cause of his panic, so severe and forbidding a look, that that gentleman retired, and made for the junior study.


The atmosphere in the junior study was close, and heavy with a blend of several strange odours. Plunkett went to the window. Then he noticed what he had never noticed before, that there were no bars to the window. Only the glass stood between him and the outer world. He threw up the sash as far as it would go. There was plenty of room to get out. So he got out. He stood for a moment inhaling the fresh air. Then, taking something from his coat pocket, he dived into the shadows. An hour passed. In the study above, Dallas, surfeited with mysteries and villainy, put down his book and stretched himself.


“I say, Vaughan,” he said. “Have you settled the House gym team yet? It’s about time the list went up.”


“Eh? What?” said Vaughan, coming slowly out of his book.


Dallas repeated his question.


===Chinese===

说来也怪，那天晚上，沃德社里想到“舍监不在正好遛弯”的，并非达拉斯一人。这个念头同样打动了普伦基特，且是往好处打动。他在楼下待着不太自在。一进高年级书房，他发现本社的上四年级生加洛韦（Galloway）已捷足先登。当晚加洛韦把女管家哄得格外熨帖，获准留在社里，而不必随书房其他人等去大堂上晚自习。有人进来时，他欲将手头的书藏到桌下，却藏得欲盖弥彰，于是便朝惊慌的祸源“共同的朋友”投去如此冷峻可怖的一瞥，以致那位先生知难而退，转赴低年级书房。


低年级书房里空气闷浊，凝着几种怪味兑成的重浊气息。普伦基特走到窗前。这时他留意到一桩从前未曾留意的事：这窗上竟没有栏杆。隔开他与外部世界的，只有一层玻璃。他把窗扇推到最高。出去的余地绰绰有余。于是他出去了。他伫立片刻，吸了几口新鲜空气。然后，从外衣口袋里取出一件东西，一头扎进了暗影里。一个钟头过去了。楼上书房里，达拉斯被神秘与罪恶撑饱了，放下书，伸了个懒腰。


“我说，沃恩，”他说，“社体操队你定好了吗？名单该张贴出去了。”


“嗯？什么？”沃恩说着，慢腾腾地从书里钻出来。


达拉斯把问题重复了一遍。


===Original===

“Yes,” said Vaughan, “got it somewhere on me. Haynes, Jarvis, and myself are going in. Only, the Mutual has to stick up the list.”


It was the unwritten rule in Ward’s, as in most of the other Houses at the School, that none but the head of the House had the right of placing notices on the House board.


“I know,” said Dallas. “I’ll go and buck him up now.”


“Don’t trouble. After prayers’ll do.”


“It’s all right. No trouble. Whom did you say? Yourself, Haynes﻿—”


===Chinese===

“定了，”沃恩说，“单子在我身上揣着呢。海恩斯（Haynes）、贾维斯（Jarvis），还有我，报名参赛。只是，贴名单得由‘共同’来。”


在沃德社，一如本校大多数宿舍，有条不成文的规矩：唯有社长才有权在宿舍布告板上张贴告示。


“我知道，”达拉斯说，“我这就去催催他。”


“别费事了。晚祷之后也来得及。”


“没事。不费事。你说都有谁来着？你本人，海恩斯——”


===Original===

“And Jarvis. Not that he’s any good. But the third string never matters much, and it’ll do him good to represent the House.”


“Right. I’ll go and unearth the Mutual.”


The result was that Galloway received another shock to his system.


“Don’t glare, Galloway. It’s rude,” said Dallas.


“Where’s Plunkett got to?” he added.


===Chinese===

“还有贾维斯。倒不是他有什么本事。不过第三号人物无足轻重，让他代表一下宿舍，对他也有好处。”


“好。我去把‘共同’掘出来。”


结果是，加洛韦又遭受了一回灵魂的震荡。


“别瞪眼，加洛韦。不礼貌，”达拉斯说。


“普伦基特上哪儿去了？”他又问。


===Original===

“Junior study,” said Galloway.


Dallas went to the junior study. There were Plunkett’s books on the table, but of their owner no signs were to be seen. The Mutual Friend had had the good sense to close the window after he had climbed through it, and Dallas did not suspect what had actually happened. He returned to Vaughan.


“The Mutual isn’t in either of the studies,” he said. “I didn’t want to spend the evening playing hide-and-seek with him, so I’ve come back.”


“It doesn’t matter, thanks all the same. Later on’ll do just as well.”


“Do you object to the window going up?” asked Dallas. “There’s a bit of a froust on in here.”


===Chinese===

“低年级书房，”加洛韦说。


达拉斯去了低年级书房。桌上摆着普伦基特的书，其主人却杳无踪影。“共同的朋友”爬出窗子后尚有先见之明，随手关了窗，达拉斯便没疑心实际发生的事。他回到了沃恩那里。


“两间书房里都没有‘共同’，”他说，“我不想把一晚上花在跟他捉迷藏上，所以回来了。”


“不打紧，还是谢谢你。晚点儿贴也一样。”


“你介意把窗子推上去吗？”达拉斯问，“这屋里有点焖味。”


===Original===

“Rather not. Heave it up.”


Dallas hove it. He stood leaning out, looking towards the College buildings, which stood out black and clear against the April sky. From out of the darkness in the direction of Stapleton sounded the monotonous note of a corncrake.


“Jove,” he said, “it’s a grand night. If I was at home now I shouldn’t be cooped up indoors like this.”


“Holidays in another week,” said Vaughan, joining him. “It is ripping, isn’t it? There’s something not half bad in the Coll. buildings on a night like this. I shall be jolly sorry to leave, in spite of Ward and the Mutual.”


“Same here, by Jove. We’ve each got a couple more years, though, if it comes to that. Hullo, prep’s over.”


===Chinese===

“完全不介意。推吧。”


达拉斯推了上去。他凭窗而立，朝校舍的方向望去。四月夜空下，校舍的轮廓黑亮分明。斯塔普尔顿方向的黑暗深处，传来秧鸡单调的啼声。


“天哪，”他说，“好一个壮丽的夜晚。这会儿要是在家里，我就不用这样关在屋里了。”


“再一星期就放假了，”沃恩说着凑了过来，“真美，是不是？这样的夜晚，咱这校舍还真有几分韵味。尽管有沃德和‘共同’，走的时候我还是会挺舍不得的。”


“我也一样，天晓得。话说回来，真要算起来，咱们各自都还有两年可待呢。喂，晚自习结束了。”


===Original===

The sound of footsteps began to be heard from the direction of the College. Nine had struck from the School clock, and the Great Hall was emptying.


“Your turn to read at prayers, Vaughan. Hullo, there’s the Mutual. Didn’t hear him unlock the door. Glad he has, though. Saves us trouble.”


“I must be going down to look up a bit to read. Do you remember when Harper read the same bit six days running? I shall never forget Ward’s pained expression. Harper explained that he thought the passage so beautiful that he couldn’t leave it.”


“Why don’t you try that tip?”


“Hardly. My reputation hasn’t quite the stamina for the test.”


===Chinese===

校舍方向开始传来脚步声。学校的大钟已敲过九点，大堂正在退场。


“轮到你在晚祷上诵读了，沃恩。喂，‘共同’回来了。没听见他开门。不过他倒是开了，省了我们的事。”


“我得下楼找一段诵读的材料。你记得哈珀（Harper）连着六天读同一段的事吗？沃德那副痛心的表情我永远忘不了。哈珀解释说，他觉得那段文字太美了，舍不得撒手。”


“你怎么不试试那个妙招？”


“还是免了。我的名声还熬不住那种考验。”


===Original===

Vaughan left the room. At the foot of the stairs he was met by the matron.


“Will you unlock the door, please, Vaughan,” she said, handing him a bunch of keys. “The boys will be coming in in a minute.”


“Unlock the door?” repeated Vaughan. “I thought it was unlocked. All right.”


“By Jove,” he thought, “the plot thickens. What is our only Plunkett doing out of the House when the door is locked, I wonder.”


Plunkett strolled in with the last batch of the returning crowd, wearing on his face the virtuous look of one who has been snatching a whiff of fresh air after a hard evening’s preparation.


===Chinese===

沃恩出了门。在楼梯脚下，他迎面碰上女管家。


“麻烦你把门打开好吗，沃恩，”她说着递过一串钥匙，“孩子们马上要回来了。”


“开门？”沃恩重复道，“我还以为开着呢。好吧。”


“好家伙，”他心想，“剧情愈发扑朔迷离了。咱这位独一无二的普伦基特，门锁着的时候待在社外，究竟在忙什么呢。”


普伦基特随着归来人流的最后一拨悠然而入，脸上带着一副正人君子的神色，好似苦读一晚之后偷空呼吸了几口新鲜空气的人。


===Original===

“Oh, I say, Plunkett,” said Vaughan, when they met in the study after prayers, “I wanted to see you. Where have you been?”


“I have been in the junior study. Where did you think I had been?”


“Oh.”


“Do you doubt my word?”


“I’ve the most exaggerated respect for your word, but you weren’t in the junior study at five to nine.”


===Chinese===

“哦，我说，普伦基特，”晚祷后两人在书房碰面，沃恩说，“我正找你呢。你上哪儿去了？”


“我一直在低年级书房。你以为我去哪儿了？”


“哦。”


“你怀疑我的话？”


“我对阁下的话抱有最夸张的敬意，可九点差五分那会儿，你可不在低年级书房。”


===Original===

“No, I went up to my dormitory about that time. You seem remarkably interested in my movements.”


“Only wanted to see you about the House gym team. You might shove up the list tonight. Haynes, Jarvis, and myself.”


“Very well.”


“I didn’t say anything to him,” said Vaughan to Dallas as they were going to their dormitories, “but, you know, there’s something jolly fishy about the Mutual. That door wasn’t unlocked when we saw him outside. I unlocked it myself. Seems to me the Mutual’s been having a little private bust of his own on the quiet.”


“That’s rum. He might have been out by the front way to see one of the beaks, though.”


===Chinese===

“不在。那会儿我上宿舍去了。你对我的行踪未免关心得过头了。”


“只是为社体操队的事找你。今晚你不妨把名单贴出去。海恩斯、贾维斯，还有我。”


“好吧。”


“我没跟他多说什么，”去宿舍的路上沃恩对达拉斯说，“不过你要知道，‘共同’身上有股说不出的腥味。我们看见他在外面那会儿，门还没开。门是我亲手开的。依我看，‘共同’一直在偷偷摸摸地自己寻欢作乐来着。”


“这就怪了。不过他也可能是走正门出去见哪位老师了。”


===Original===

“Well, even then he would be breaking rules. You aren’t allowed to go out after lockup without House beak’s leave. No, I find him guilty.”


“If only he’d go and get booked!” said Vaughan. “Then he might have to leave. But he won’t. No such luck.”


“No,” said Dallas. “Good night.”


“Good night.”


Certainly there was something mysterious about the matter.


===Chinese===

“嗯，就算那样，他也在犯规。锁门之后，未经舍监准假，任何人不得外出。不，我判他有罪。”


“他要肯去犯个够记过的错就好了！”沃恩说，“那样他就许得滚蛋了。可他不会。没这好运。”


“不会的，”达拉斯说，“晚安。”


“晚安。”


此事确有几分神秘。

## VI / 六

===Original===

Literary Banquet


Charteris and Welch were conversing in the study of which they were the joint proprietors. That is to say, Charteris was talking and playing the banjo alternately, while Welch was deep in a book and refused to be drawn out of it under any pretext. Charteris’ banjo was the joy of his fellows and the bane of his Housemaster. Being of a musical turn and owning a good deal of pocket money, he had, at the end of the summer holidays, introduced the delights of a phonograph into the House. This being vetoed by the Housemaster, he had returned at the beginning of the following term with a penny whistle, which had suffered a similar fate. Upon this he had invested in a banjo, and the dazed Merevale, feeling that matters were getting beyond his grip, had effected a compromise with him. Having ascertained that there was no specific rule at St. Austin’s against the use of musical instruments, he had informed Charteris that if he saw fit to play the banjo before prep only, and regarded the hours between seven and eleven as a close time, all should be forgiven, and he might play, if so disposed, till the crack of doom. To this reasonable request Charteris had promptly acceded, and peace had been restored. Charteris and Welch were a curious pair. Welch spoke very little. Charteris was seldom silent. They were both in the Sixth﻿—Welch high up, Charteris rather low down. In games, Welch was one of those fortunate individuals who are good at everything. He was captain of cricket, and not only captain, but also the best all-round man in the team, which is often a very different matter. He was the best wing three-quarter the School possessed; played fives and racquets like a professor, and only the day before had shared Tony’s glory by winning the silver medal for fencing in the Aldershot competition.


The abilities of Charteris were more ordinary. He was a sound bat, and went in first for the Eleven, and played half for the Fifteen. As regards work, he might have been brilliant if he had chosen, but his energies were mainly devoted to the compilation of a monthly magazine (strictly unofficial) entitled *The Glow Worm*. This he edited, and for the most part wrote himself. It was a clever periodical, and rarely failed to bring him in at least ten shillings per number, after deducting the expenses which the College bookseller, who acted as sole agent, did his best to make as big as possible. Only a very few of the elect knew the identity of the editor, and they were bound to strict secrecy. On the day before the publication of each number, a notice was placed in the desk of the captain of each form, notifying him of what the morrow would bring forth, and asking him to pass it round the form. That was all. The School did the rest. *The Glow Worm* always sold well, principally because of the personal nature of its contents. If the average mortal is told that there is something about him in a paper, he will buy that paper at your own price.


Today he was giving his monthly tea in honour of the new number. Only contributors were invited, and the menu was always of the best. It was a *Punch* dinner, only more so, for these teas were celebrated with musical honours, and Charteris on the banjo was worth hearing. His rendering of extracts from the works of Messrs. Gilbert and Sullivan was an intellectual treat.


“When I take the chair at our harmonic club!” he chanted, fixing the unconscious Welch with a fiery glance. “Welch!”


===Chinese===

文人雅宴


查特里斯和韦尔奇正在两人合有的书房里交谈。说是交谈，其实是查特里斯一边说话一边轮流弹班卓琴，而韦尔奇埋头于一本厚书，任凭什么借口也不肯被拽出来。查特里斯的班卓琴是他的伙伴们的欢乐、他的舍监的灾星。他生性爱好音乐，零花钱又充裕，暑假结束时便把留声机（phonograph）的妙趣引进了宿舍。留声机被舍监封禁后，下学期开学他又带回一支便士哨笛（penny whistle），遭遇了同样的命运。于是他转而投资了一把班卓琴。晕头转向的梅雷维尔眼见事态渐失掌控，便同他达成妥协。查明圣奥斯汀并无明文禁止使用乐器之后，梅雷维尔通知查特里斯：倘若他肯只在晚自习前弹琴，把七点到十一点视为禁猎期（close time），则既往不咎，而且只要他乐意，尽可一直弹到世界末日。对这一合情合理的要求，查特里斯慨然应允，和平随之恢复。查特里斯和韦尔奇是古怪的一对。韦尔奇寡言，查特里斯难得安静。两人同在六年级——韦尔奇名列前茅，查特里斯屈居下游。论运动，韦尔奇属于那种样样精通的幸运儿。他是板球队长，且不仅是队长，还是队里最好的全能选手——这两者往往大不相同。他是全校最好的边三分卫（wing three-quarter）；墙手球和墙拍球（racquets）打得教授一般，而就在前一天，他又在奥尔德肖特锦标赛上夺得击剑银质奖章，与托尼分享了荣耀。


查特里斯的本事则平凡些。他是位稳健的击球手，在第一板球队（the Eleven）打头阵，在十五人制橄榄球队打边锋。至于功课，他若肯下功夫本可出类拔萃，可惜精力大多用于编一份月刊（严格非官方），名为《萤火虫》（*The Glow Worm*）。他任主编，且大部分稿件亲自捉刀。这是份聪明的刊物，每期扣除费用后少说也能给他带来十先令进账——而费用一项，由独家代理的校内书商竭力做大。主编的身份只有极少数圈内人知晓，且都受严守秘密之约束。每期出版前一日，各班班长的书桌里会被放进一张通知，告知明日将有何等惊喜，并请其在班上传阅。如此而已。其余交由全校办理。《萤火虫》向来畅销，主要归功于内容的人身属性。寻常凡人若听说某报上有关于他的文字，你开什么价他都买。


这天他正设他的月度茶会，以庆祝新一期出刊。来宾仅限撰稿人，茶点向来尽善尽美。这是《笨拙》（*Punch*）式的晚宴，且有过之，因为这等茶会照例伴以音乐助兴，而查特里斯的班卓琴值得一听。他演绎吉尔伯特与沙利文（Gilbert and Sullivan）二氏作品选段，堪称一场智识的盛宴。


“当我在咱和声俱乐部主持会议（When I take the chair at our harmonic club）！”他高唱道，目光灼灼地钉着浑然不觉的韦尔奇。“韦尔奇！”


===Original===

“Yes.”


“If this is your idea of a harmonic club, it isn’t mine. Put down that book, and try and be sociable.”


“One second,” said Welch, burrowing still deeper.


“That’s what you always say,” said Charteris. “Look here﻿—Come in.”


There had been a knock at the door as he was speaking. Tony entered, accompanied by Jim. They were regular attendants at these banquets, for between them they wrote most of what was left of the magazine when Charteris had done with it. There was only one other contributor, Jackson, of Dawson’s House, and he came in a few minutes later. Welch was the athletics expert of the paper, and did most of the match reports.


===Chinese===

“嗯。”


“如果你对和声俱乐部的想象是这样，那可不是我的。放下书，学着合群点。”


“稍等，”韦尔奇说着，往书里陷得更深。


“你老说稍等，”查特里斯道。“听着——请进。”


说话间有人敲门。托尼进来了，身后跟着吉姆。二人是这类宴会的常客，因为查特里斯写完之后杂志剩下的部分，大半出自他们笔下。撰稿人只剩另一位，道森社（Dawson's）的杰克逊（Jackson），几分钟后也到了。韦尔奇是刊物的田径专家，大部分比赛报道都出自他手。


===Original===

“Now we’re complete,” said Charteris, as Jackson presented himself. “Gentlemen﻿—your seats. There are only four chairs, and we, as Wordsworth might have said, but didn’t, are five. All right, I’ll sit on the table. Welch, you worm, away with melancholy. Take away his book, somebody. That’s right. Who says what? Tea already made. Coffee published shortly. If anybody wants cocoa, I’ve got some, only you’ll have to boil more water. I regret the absence of menu cards, but as the entire feast is visible to the naked eye, our loss is immaterial. The offertory will be for the Church expenses fund. Biscuits, please.”


“I wish you’d given this tea after next Saturday, Alderman,” said Jim. Charteris was called the Alderman on account of his figure, which was inclined to stoutness, and his general capacity for consuming food.


“Never put off till tomorrow﻿—Why?”


“I simply must keep fit for the mile. How’s Welch to run, too, if he eats this sort of thing?” He pointed to the well-spread board.


“Yes, there’s something in that,” said Tony. “Thank goodness, my little entertainment’s over. I think I *will* try one of those chocolate things. Thanks.”


===Chinese===

“这下齐了，”杰克逊一到，查特里斯便说。“诸位——各就各位。椅子只有四把，而我们——正如华兹华斯（Wordsworth）可能说过而没说的——是五个。行，我坐桌上。韦尔奇，你这懒虫，把忧郁收起来。来人，把他的书没收。这就对了。谁要点什么？茶已沏好。咖啡稍后印行。谁想要可可，我这儿有，只是得再烧点水。没有菜单卡，深表遗憾，好在整席盛宴肉眼可见，损失无关紧要。奉献金（offertory）将拨充教会经费基金。请递饼干。”


“真希望你把这茶会挪到下周六以后再办，市政官（Alderman），”吉姆说。查特里斯之所以被称作市政官，是因为他体形发福，且饭量惊人。


“今日事今日毕，切莫拖延——为什么？”


“我就是得为一英里保持状态。再说韦尔奇要是吃了这类东西，还怎么跑？”他指了指琳琅满目的茶桌。


“是，这话在理，”托尼说，“谢天谢地，我那点小小消遣已经完事了。我想我*确实*要尝一块那种巧克力玩意儿。多谢。”


===Original===

“Welch is all right,” said Jackson. “He could win the hundred and the quarter on sausage rolls. But think of the times.”


“And there,” observed Charteris, “there, my young friend, you have touched upon a sore subject. Before you came in I was administering a few wholesome words of censure to that miserable object on your right. What is a fifth of a second more or less that it should make a man insult his digestion as Welch does? You’ll hardly credit it, but for the last three weeks or more I have been forced to look on a fellow being refusing pastry and drinking beastly extracts of meat, all for the sake of winning a couple of races. It quite put me off my feed. Cake, please. Good robust slice. Thanks.”


“It’s rather funny when you come to think of it,” said Tony. “Welch lives on Bovril for, a month, and then, just as he thinks he’s going to score, a burglar with a sense of humour strolls into the Pav., carefully selects the only two cups he had a chance of winning, and so to bed.”


“Leaving Master J. G. Welch an awful example of what comes of training,” said Jim. “Welch, you’re a rotter.”


“It isn’t *my* fault,” observed Welch, plaintively. “You chaps seem to think I’ve committed some sort of crime, just because a man I didn’t know from Adam has bagged a cup or two.”


===Chinese===

“韦尔奇没事，”杰克逊说，“靠香肠卷他也能拿下百码和四分之一英里。可成绩呢，成绩怎么办。”


“说到这个，”查特里斯评点道，“说到这个，我年轻的朋友，你可戳着痛处了。你们进来之前，我正对右边那位可怜人施以几句有益的训诫。多那五分之一秒少那五分之一秒，算得了什么，值得一个男人像韦尔奇这样糟蹋自己的消化机能？你简直不会相信，三个多星期以来，我被迫眼睁睁看着一个活人拒绝糕点、灌些可怕的肉精汤，全为了赢两场赛跑。看得我饭都吃不下了。蛋糕，麻烦。厚实一片。谢谢。”


“细想起来还真有点滑稽，”托尼说，“韦尔奇喝了一个月保维利（Bovril），正当他以为要得手时，一个有幽默感的窃贼踱进休息亭，精挑细选了他唯一有机会赢的那两座奖杯，然后——如此上床睡觉。”


“给 J. G. 韦尔奇小少爷留下一堂关于训练后果的可怕教材，”吉姆说，“韦尔奇，你是个恶棍。”


“这可不怪*我*，”韦尔奇委屈地说。“就因为一个我素不相识的家伙顺走了一两座杯子，你们几位倒好像认定我犯了什么罪似的。”


===Original===

“It looks to me,” said Charteris, “as if Welch, thinking his chances of the quarter rather rocky, hired one of his low acquaintances to steal the cup for him.”


“Shouldn’t wonder. Welch knows some jolly low characters in Stapleton.”


“Welch is a jolly low character himself,” said Tony, judicially. “I wonder you associate with him, Alderman.”


“Stand *in loco parentis*. Aunt of his asked me to keep an eye on him. ‘Dear George is so wild,’ she said.”


Before Welch could find words to refute this hideous slander, Tony cut in once more.


===Chinese===

“依我看，”查特里斯说，“事情像是这样：韦尔奇掂量着自己四分之一英里的胜算不太稳，便雇了个下三滥熟人替他把杯子偷了。”


“不足为奇。韦尔奇在斯塔普尔顿认识的下三滥可不少。”


“韦尔奇本人就是个不折不扣的下三滥，”托尼以法官口吻判道，“真奇怪你居然同他交往，市政官。”


“我这是*代行家长之责*（in loco parentis）。他姑母托我照看他。‘亲爱的乔治（George）太野了，’她说。”


韦尔奇还没想出话来驳斥这无耻的诽谤，托尼又插了进来。


===Original===

“The only reason he doesn’t drink gin and play billiards at the Blue Lion is that gin makes him ill and his best break at pills is six, including two flukes.”


“As a matter of fact,” said Welch, changing the conversation with a jerk, “I don’t much care if the cups are stolen. One doesn’t only run for the sake of the pot.”


Charteris groaned. “Oh, well,” said he, “if you’re going to take the high moral standpoint, and descend to brazen platitudes like that, I give you up.”


“It’s a rum thing about those pots,” said Welch, meditatively.


“Seems to me,” Jim rejoined, “the rum thing is that a man who considers the Pav. a safe place to keep a lot of valuable prizes in should be allowed at large. Why couldn’t they keep them in the board room as they used to?”


===Chinese===

“他之所以没在蓝狮酒馆（Blue Lion）喝杜松子酒、打台球，唯一的原因是杜松子酒叫他反胃，而他打台球（pills）的最好连击纪录是六分，其中还有两杆是侥幸。”


“说实在的，”韦尔奇猛地岔开话题，“杯子被偷我并不怎么在乎。人跑步又不光是为那只奖杯（pot）。”


查特里斯一声呻吟。“唉，得，”他说，“你要是打算站上道德高地，堕落到说出这等厚颜的陈词滥调，我就不管你了。”


“杯子这事真有点邪门，”韦尔奇若有所思地说。


“依我看，”吉姆接话，“真正邪门的是：一个认为休息亭适合存放大批贵重奖品的人，居然还能放出来自由活动。他们怎么不像从前那样放在会议室？”


===Original===

“Thought it ’ud save trouble, I suppose. Save them carting the things over to the Pav. on Sports Day,” hazarded Tony.


“Saved the burglar a lot of trouble, I should say,” observed Jackson. “I could break into the Pav. myself in five minutes.”


“Good old Jackson,” said Charteris, “have a shot tonight. I’ll hold the watch. I’m doing a leader on the melancholy incident for next month’s *Glow Worm*. It appears that Master Reginald Robinson, a member of Mr. Merevale’s celebrated boarding establishment, was passing by the Pavilion at an early hour on the morning of the second of April﻿—that’s today﻿—when his eye was attracted by an excavation or incision in one of the windows of that imposing edifice. His narrative appears on another page. Interviewed by a *Glow Worm* representative, Master Robinson, who is a fine, healthy, bronzed young Englishman of some thirteen summers, with a delightful, boyish flow of speech, not wholly free from a suspicion of cheek, gave it as his opinion that the outrage was the work of a burglar﻿—a remarkable display of sagacity in one so young. A portrait of Master Robinson appears on another page.”


“Everything seems to appear on another page,” said Jim. “Am I to do the portrait?”


“I think it would be best. You can never trust a photo to caricature a person enough. Your facial H.B.’s the thing.”


===Chinese===

“我猜是图省事吧。省得运动会那天再把东西拖到休息亭去，”托尼猜测道。


“要我说，是给窃贼省了不少事，”杰克逊评道。“换了我，五分钟就能闯进休息亭。”


“好个杰克逊，”查特里斯说，“今晚就去露一手吧。我给你掐表。下月《萤火虫》的社论我就写这件惨案。据说，雷金纳德·罗宾逊小少爷——梅雷维尔先生那所著名寄宿学府的在册学员——于四月二日（也就是今天）清晨路过休息亭时，目光被那座宏伟建筑某扇窗户上的一个洞口或曰切口所吸引。其叙述详见另页。本刊记者专访时，罗宾逊小少爷——一位约莫十三岁、晒得黝黑、健壮俊朗的英国少年，谈吐淋漓而有童趣，且不无鲁莽之嫌——表示他认定此暴行系窃贼所为。小小年纪便有如此非凡的明断，令人叹服。罗宾逊小少爷之玉照详见另页。”


“什么都是详见另页，”吉姆说，“画像也归我画？”


“我想这样最好。照片这东西，你永远不能指望它把人丑化到位。你那张脸就是现成的 H.B. 铅笔。”


===Original===

“Have you heard whether anything else was bagged besides the cups?” asked Welch.


“Not that I know of,” said Jim.


“Yes there was,” said Jackson. “It further appears that that lunatic, Adamson, had left some money in the pocket of his blazer, which he had left in the Pav. overnight. On enquiry it was found that the money had also left.”


Adamson was in the same House as Jackson, and had talked of nothing else throughout the whole of lunch. He was an abnormally wealthy individual, however, and it was generally felt, though he himself thought otherwise, that he could afford to lose some of the surplus.


“How much?” asked Jim.


===Chinese===

“除了杯子，还听说丢了别的什么没有？”韦尔奇问。


“据我所知没有，”吉姆说。


“有，”杰克逊说。“进一步的消息是：那个疯子亚当森（Adamson）在上衣口袋里留了些钱，而那件上衣在休息亭里过了一夜。经查证，钱也已不辞而别。”


亚当森和杰克逊同社，午饭时从头到尾净说这件事。不过他家境异常殷实，众人都觉得他损失点余财无伤大雅——他自己倒不这么想。


“多少？”吉姆问。


===Original===

“Two pounds.”


At this Jim gave vent to the exclamation which Mr. Barry Pain calls the Englishman’s shortest prayer.


“My dear sir,” said Charteris. “My very dear sir. We blush for you. Might I ask *why* you take the matter to heart so?”


Jim hesitated.


“Better have it out, Jim,” said Tony. “These chaps’ll keep it dark all right.” And Jim entered once again upon the recital of his doings on the previous night.


===Chinese===

“两镑。”


闻听此言，吉姆发出一声巴里·佩因（Barry Pain）先生所谓的“英国人最短的祷文”。


“亲爱的先生，”查特里斯说，“非常亲爱的先生。我们为你脸红。可否请问*为何*你把这事如此挂在心上？”


吉姆迟疑了。


“还是说了吧，吉姆，”托尼说，“这几位嘴严得很。”于是吉姆又一次开始讲述前一晚的所作所为。


===Original===

“So you see,” he concluded, “this two pound business makes it all the worse.”


“I don’t see why,” said Welch.


“Well, you see, money’s a thing everybody wants, whereas cups wouldn’t be any good to a fellow at school. So that I should find it much harder to prove that I didn’t take the two pounds, than I should have done to prove that I didn’t take the cups.”


“But there’s no earthly need for you to prove anything,” said Tony. “There’s not the slightest chance of your being found out.”


“Exactly,” observed Charteris. “We will certainly respect your incog. if you wish it. Wild horses shall draw no evidence from us. It is, of course, very distressing, but what is man after all? Are we not as the beasts that perish, and is not our little life rounded by a sleep? Indeed, yes. And now﻿—with full chorus, please.


===Chinese===

“所以你们明白，”他总结道，“这两镑的案子让事情更糟。”


“我不明白为什么，”韦尔奇说。


“嗐，你瞧，钱这东西人人想要，杯子呢，对在校学生毫无用处。所以，证明我没拿那两镑，要比证明我没拿杯子难得多。”


“可你压根无须证明什么，”托尼说，“你被查出来的可能性微乎其微。”


“正是，”查特里斯评道，“你若愿意隐姓埋名，我们必定尊重。野马也休想从我们嘴里拖出半句证词。这固然令人痛心，可人到底是什么呢？我们岂不正如将亡的走兽，区区一生不就以一觉收场？诚哉斯言。现在——请齐声合唱。


===Original===

“We-e take him from the city or the plough.


					We-e dress him up in uniform so ne-e-e-at.”


And at the third line some plaster came down from the ceiling, and Merevale came up, and the meeting dispersed without the customary cheers.


===Chinese===

“我——们把他从城里或犁头带走。


“我——们给他穿上制服，整——整——齐齐。”


唱到第三句时，天花板掉下一块灰皮，梅雷维尔上了楼，集会未及照例欢呼便宣告解散。

## VII / 七

===Original===

Barrett Explores


Barrett stood at the window of his study with his hands in his pockets, looking thoughtfully at the football field. Now and then he whistled. That was to show that he was very much at his ease. He whistled a popular melody of the day three times as slowly as its talented composer had originally intended it to be whistled, and in a strange minor key. Some people, when offended, invariably whistle in this manner, and these are just the people with whom, if you happen to share a study with them, it is rash to have differences of opinion. Reade, who was deep in a book﻿—though not so deep as he would have liked the casual observer to fancy him to be﻿—would have given much to stop Barrett’s musical experiments. To ask him to stop in so many words was, of course, impossible. Offended dignity must draw the line somewhere. That is one of the curious results of a polite education. When two gentlemen of Hoxton or the Borough have a misunderstanding, they address one another with even more freedom than is their usual custom. When one member of a public school falls out with another member, his politeness in dealing with him becomes so Chesterfieldian, that one cannot help being afraid that he will sustain a strain from which he will never recover.


After a time the tension became too much for Barrett. He picked up his cap and left the room. Reade continued to be absorbed in his book.


It was a splendid day outside, warm for April, and with just that freshness in the air which gets into the blood and makes spring the best time of the whole year. Barrett had not the aesthetic soul to any appreciable extent, but he did know a fine day when he saw one, and even he realised that a day like this was not to be wasted in pottering about the School grounds watching the “under thirteen” hundred yards (trial heats) and the “under fourteen” broad jump, or doing occasional exercises in the gymnasium. It was a day for going far afield and not returning till lockup. He had an object, too. Everything seemed to shout “eggs” at him, to remind him that he was an enthusiast on the subject and had a collection to which he ought to seize this excellent opportunity of adding. The only question was, where to go. The surrounding country was a paradise for the naturalist who had no absurd scruples on the subject of trespassing. To the west, in the direction of Stapleton, the woods and hedges were thick with nests. But then, so they were to the east along the Badgwick road. He wavered, but a recollection that there was water in the Badgwick direction, and that he might with luck beard a water wagtail in its lair, decided him. What is life without a water wagtail’s egg? A mere mockery. He turned east.


“Hullo, Barrett, where are you off to?” Grey, of Prater’s House, intercepted him as he was passing.


===Chinese===

巴雷特探穴


巴雷特双手插兜站在书房窗前，若有所思地望着足球场。他不时吹吹口哨。这是为了表明自己安然自得。他把当日一首流行小调，用比那位才华横溢的作曲家原先设想的慢三倍的速度，配着一个古怪的小调吹出来。有些人一旦生了气，吹口哨一律是这个路数；而恰恰是这种人，你若碰巧与之合用一间书房，同他们闹意见分歧便是轻率之举。里德正埋首书中——只是不及他乐意让漫不经心的旁观者以为的那样深——他情愿付出很大代价叫停巴雷特的音乐实验。用明明白白的话求他停下，当然不可能。受了冒犯的尊严总得在某处划下界线。这便是斯文教育古怪的结果之一。霍克斯顿（Hoxton）或巴勒（the Borough）的两位绅士起了龃龉，彼此说话会比平素更加无所顾忌；而一个公学学生与另一个闹翻之后，待对方的客气会变得如此切斯特菲尔德（Chesterfield）式，叫人不禁担心他承受的张力将终身不得复原。


过了一阵，这份张力让巴雷特也撑不住了。他抓起帽子出了屋。里德继续沉浸在他的书里。


屋外是个大好天，就四月而言称得上暖和，空气中正有一种沁入血液的清新，使春天成为全年最佳时节。巴雷特谈不上有多少审美灵魂，但好天气他还是识货的，连他也明白，这样的日子不该浪费在学校里瞎转，看“十三岁以下”百码预赛和“十四岁以下”跳远，或到体育馆偶尔练两下。这是出远门、不到锁门不回来的日子。何况他还有目标。万物仿佛都冲他高喊“鸟蛋”，提醒他自己是这项事业的狂热爱好者，藏有一批藏品，理当抓住这天赐良机予以扩充。唯一的问题是去哪儿。周边乡野，对于在“擅入私地”问题上毫无迂腐顾忌的博物学家而言，是一方天堂。往西，斯塔普尔顿方向，树林和树篱里鸟巢密布。可往东，巴奇威克路沿线，同样如此。他犹豫不决，忽然想起巴奇威克方向有水，运气好的话或许能直捣鹡鸰（water wagtail）的老巢，这便下了决心。人生若没有一枚鹡鸰蛋，算什么人生？徒然一场笑柄。他转向东去。


“喂，巴雷特，上哪儿去？”普拉特社（Prater's）的格雷（Grey）在他经过时拦住了他。


===Original===

“Going to see if I can get some eggs. Are you coming?”


Grey hesitated. He was a keen naturalist, too.


“No, I don’t think I will, thanks. Got an uncle coming down to see me.”


“Well, cut off before he comes.”


“No, he’d be too sick. Besides,” he added, ingenuously, “there’s a possible tip. Don’t want to miss that. I’m simply stony. Always am at end of term.”


===Chinese===

“去看看能不能掏几个蛋。你去吗？”


格雷犹豫了。他也是位热切的博物学家。


“我看就不去了，谢谢。有位叔叔要来看我。”


“那趁他来之前溜呗。”


“不行，他会很不痛快的。再说，”他坦然补了一句，“说不定有赏钱。可不能错过。我简直一个子儿都没有。学期末总是这样。”


===Original===

“Oh,” said Barrett, realising that further argument would be thrown away. “Well, so long, then.”


“So long. Hope you have luck.”


“Thanks. I say.”


“Well?”


“Roll-call, you know. If you don’t see me anywhere about, you might answer my name.”


===Chinese===

“哦，”巴雷特说，明白再争也是白费口舌。“那回见。”


“回见。祝你好运。”


“谢了。我说。”


“怎么？”


“点名这事儿，你知道的。要是四下里见不着我，麻烦你替我应个名。”


===Original===

“All right. And if you find anything decent, you might remember me. You know pretty well what I’ve got already.”


“Right, I will.”


“Magpie’s what I want particularly. Where are you going, by the way?”


“Thought of having a shot at old Venner’s woods. I’m after a water wagtail myself. Ought to be one or two in the Dingle.”


“Heaps, probably. But I should advise you to look out, you know. Venner’s awfully down on trespassing.”


===Chinese===

“行。还有，你要找着什么好东西，惦记着我点儿。我手上有什么你基本清楚。”


“好，一定。”


“喜鹊（magpie）蛋是我特别想要的。对了，你打算去哪儿？”


“想去老文纳的林子碰碰运气。我自己就冲着鹡鸰去的。丁格尔幽谷（the Dingle）里该有一两只。”


“多半一大堆。不过我劝你当心，知道吗。文纳对擅入私地深恶痛绝。”


===Original===

“Yes, the bounder. But I don’t think he’ll get me. One gets the knack of keeping fairly quiet with practice.”


“He’s got thousands of keepers.”


“Millions.”


“Dogs, too.”


“Dash his beastly dogs. I like dogs. Why are you such a croaker today, Grey?”


===Chinese===

“可不是，那无赖。可我不认为他抓得着我。练得多了，自然摸到悄然行事的诀窍。”


“他养着成千的看守。”


“上万的。”


“还有狗。”


“去他的狗。我喜欢狗。你今天怎么这么乌鸦嘴，格雷？”


===Original===

“Well, you know he’s had two chaps sacked for going in his woods to my certain knowledge, Morton-Smith and Ainsworth. That’s only since I’ve been at the Coll., too. Probably lots more before that.”


“Ainsworth was booked smoking there. That’s why he was sacked. And Venner caught Morton-Smith himself simply staggering under dead rabbits. They sack any chap for poaching.”


“Well, I don’t see how you’re going to show you’ve not been poaching. Besides, it’s miles out of bounds.”


“Grey,” said Barrett, severely, “I’m surprised at you. Go away and meet your beastly uncle. Fancy talking about bounds at your time of life.”


“Well, don’t forget me when you’re hauling in the eggs.”


===Chinese===

“呃，你知道，我确切知道就有两个人因为进他的林子被开革，莫顿-史密斯和安斯沃思（Ainsworth）。这还只是我来公学之后的事。之前保不齐还有更多。”


“安斯沃思是在那儿抽烟被抓了记过。那才被开革的。文纳还亲手逮住过莫顿-史密斯，当时他扛着死兔子都快压趴下了。偷猎的人，他们见一个开一个。”


“可我不明白，你怎么证明自己没偷猎。再说，那儿出校界好几英里呢。”


“格雷，”巴雷特严肃地说，“你让我失望。走开，见你那讨厌的叔叔去吧。你这把年纪了，居然还谈什么校界。”


“行，等你满载而归时可别忘了我。”


===Original===

“Right you are. So long.”


Barrett proceeded on his way, his last difficulty safely removed. He could rely on Grey not to bungle that matter of roll-call. Grey had been there before.


A long white ribbon of dusty road separated St. Austin’s from the lodge gates of Badgwick Hall, the country seat of Sir Alfred Venner, M.P., also of 49A Lancaster Gate, London. Barrett walked rapidly for over half an hour before he came in sight of the great iron gates, flanked on the one side by a trim little lodge and green meadows, and on the other by woods of a darker green. Having got so far, he went on up the hill till at last he arrived at his destination. A small hedge, a sloping strip of green, and then the famous Dingle. I am loath to inflict any scenic rhapsodies on the reader, but really the Dingle deserves a line or two. It was the most beautiful spot in a country noted for its fine scenery. Dense woods were its chief feature. And by dense I mean well supplied not only with trees (excellent things in themselves, but for the most part useless to the nest hunter), but also with a fascinating tangle of undergrowth, where every bush seemed to harbour eggs. All carefully preserved, too. That was the chief charm of the place. Since the sad episodes of Morton-Smith and Ainsworth, the School for the most part had looked askance at the Dingle. Once a select party from Dacre’s House, headed by Babington, who always got himself into hot water when possible, had ventured into the forbidden land, and had returned hurriedly later in the afternoon with every sign of exhaustion, hinting breathlessly at keepers, dogs, and a pursuit that had lasted fifty minutes without a check. Since then no one had been daring enough to brave the terrors so carefully prepared for them by Milord Sir Venner and his minions, and the proud owner of the Dingle walked his woods in solitary state. Occasionally he would personally conduct some favoured guest thither and show him the wonders of the place. But this was not a frequent occurrence. On still-less frequent occasions, there were large shooting parties in the Dingle. But, as a rule, the word was “Keepers only. No others need apply.”


A futile iron railing, some three feet in height, shut in the Dingle. Barrett jumped this lightly, and entered forthwith into paradise. The place was full of nests. As Barrett took a step forward there was a sudden whirring of wings, and a bird rose from a bush close beside him. He went to inspect, and found a nest with seven eggs in it. Only a thrush, of course. As no one ever wants thrushes’ eggs the world is overstocked with them. Still, it gave promise of good things to come. Barrett pushed on through the bushes and the promise was fulfilled. He came upon another nest. Five eggs this time, of a variety he was unable with his moderate knowledge to classify. At any rate, he had not got them in his collection. Nor, to the best of his belief, had Grey. He took one for each of them.


Now this was all very well, thought Barrett, but what he had come for was the ovular deposit of the water wagtail. Through the trees he could see the silver gleam of the brook at the foot of the hill. The woods sloped down to the very edge. Then came the brook, widening out here into the size of a small river. Then woods again all up the side of the opposite hill. Barrett hurried down the slope.


===Chinese===

“忘不了。回见。”


巴雷特继续赶路，最后一道难关已安然排除。点名的事，格雷不会办砸，这点他信得过。这种事格雷先前也历练过。


一条白尘仆仆的漫长路带，把圣奥斯汀与巴奇威克庄园的门房大门隔开——那是议员阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士的乡间宅邸，他在伦敦兰开斯特门 49A 号（49A Lancaster Gate）另有居所。巴雷特疾走了半个多小时，才望见那两扇大铁门：一侧是齐整的小门房和绿茵草场，另一侧是色调更深的绿林。到了这里，他继续上坡，终于抵达目的地。一道矮树篱，一片倾斜的绿坡，然后便是大名鼎鼎的丁格尔幽谷。我不愿拿写景的狂想折磨读者，可丁格尔实在配得上只言片语。在一个以风光著称的乡间，它是至美之地。茂林是其主要特色。我说茂，意思是不仅树木葱茏（树本身是好东西，但对寻巢者大抵无用），而且下木纠结丛生，引人入胜，仿佛丛丛灌木都藏着鸟蛋。且一切都被精心保护着。这正是此地最大的魅力。自莫顿-史密斯与安斯沃思那两出悲剧之后，全校大体上对丁格尔侧目而视。曾有一回，达克社（Dacre's）一支精干小队在巴宾顿（Babington）——此君凡有可能必惹祸上身——率领下冒进禁地，当天下午晚些时候仓皇撤回，个个精疲力竭之状毕露，气喘吁吁地暗示有看守、有狗、有一场历时五十分钟不曾停歇的追击。自那以后，再无人有胆直面文纳爵士老爷及其爪牙精心布下的天罗地网，丁格尔骄傲的主人便独自威严地巡行林间。偶尔他会亲自引领某位受宠的宾客前去，一睹此间奇观。但这不算常有。更少有的，是丁格尔里的大规模围猎。但通常而言，通行口令是：“只限看守。余者免进。”


一道三英尺来高的铁栏杆，徒劳地围住丁格尔。巴雷特轻轻一跃而过，随即步入天堂。此地鸟巢遍布。巴雷特才迈一步，翅膀倏然一阵扑棱，一只鸟从他身旁的灌木里腾起。他上前查看，发现一巢七蛋。当然只是鸫（thrush）。由于从来没人要鸫蛋，世上已经供过于求。不过，这预示着好戏在后头。巴雷特在灌木间继续推进，预示也应验了。他又撞见一个巢。这回是五枚蛋，品种以他有限的知识无从归类。无论如何，他的藏品中没有。据他所知，格雷也没有。他给两人各取了一枚。


巴雷特心想，这些固然不错，可他此行的目标是鹡鸰的卵。透过树林，他望得见山脚下小溪的银光。林地一路斜到溪边。接着是溪流，在这里拓宽成一条小河的规模。再往前，对面的山坡上又是满山林地。巴雷特匆匆下坡。


===Original===

He had put on flannels for this emergency. He was prepared to wade, to swim if necessary. He hoped that it would not be necessary, for in April water is generally inclined to be chilly. Of keepers he had up till now seen no sign. Once he had heard the distant bark of a dog. It seemed to come from far across the stream and he had not troubled about it.


In the midst of the bushes on the bank stood a tree. It was not tall compared to the other trees of the Dingle, but standing alone as it did amongst the undergrowth it attracted the eye at once. Barrett, looking at it, saw something which made him forget water wagtails for the moment. In a fork in one of the upper branches was a nest, an enormous nest, roughly constructed of sticks. It was a very jerry-built residence, evidently run up for the season by some prudent bird who knew by experience that no nest could last through the winter, and so had declined to waste his time in useless decorative work. But what bird was it? No doubt there are experts to whom a wood pigeon’s nest is something apart and distinct from the nest of the magpie, but to your unsophisticated amateur a nest that is large may be anything﻿—rook’s, magpie’s, pigeon’s, or great auk’s. To such an one the only true test lies in the eggs. *Solvitur ambulando.* Barrett laid the pillboxes, containing the precious specimens he had found in the nest at the top of the hill, at the foot of the tree, and began to climb.


It was to be a day of surprises for him. When he had got halfway up he found himself on a kind of ledge, which appeared to be a kind of junction at which the tree branched off into two parts. To the left was the nest, high up in its fork. To the right was another shoot. He realised at once, with keen disappointment, that it would be useless to go further. The branches were obviously not strong enough to bear his weight. He looked down, preparatory to commencing the descent, and to his astonishment found himself looking into a black cavern. In his eagerness to reach the nest he had not noticed before that the tree was hollow.


This made up for a great many things. His disappointment became less keen. Few things are more interesting than a hollow tree.


“Wonder how deep it goes down,” he said to himself. He broke off a piece of wood and dropped it down the hollow. It seemed to reach the ground uncommonly soon. He tried another piece. The sound of its fall came up to him almost simultaneously. Evidently the hole was not deep. He placed his hands on the edge, and let himself gently down into the darkness. His feet touched something solid almost immediately. As far as he could judge, the depth of the cavity was not more than five feet. Standing up at his full height he could just rest his chin on the edge.


===Chinese===

他为这一突发情况穿上了法兰绒裤。他准备蹚水，必要时游泳。他希望不必如此，因为四月的水一般偏凉。至于看守，至今不见踪影。有一回他听见远处狗吠，似乎来自溪流对岸很远的地方，他也就没放在心上。


岸边灌木丛中立着一棵树。比起丁格尔其他的树，它算不得高，但独处于下木之间，一眼便夺人视线。巴雷特望着它，看到了一样东西，令他一时忘了鹡鸰。上层一根枝杈的分叉处有个巢，一个大得惊人的巢，用树枝粗粗搭成。那是座十足的偷工减料的住宅，显然是某只谨慎的鸟为当季草草赶建的——它凭经验深知没有鸟能熬过寒冬，故而不肯把时间浪费于无用的装修。可这是只什么鸟？毫无疑问，行家自能分辨斑鸠巢与喜鹊巢之天壤有别，可对你这不谙世故的业余爱好者来说，个大巢便什么都有可能——白嘴鸦的、喜鹊的、鸽子的，乃至大海雀（great auk）的。对这种人，唯一可靠的检验在蛋。*Solvitur ambulando*（走着便见分晓）。巴雷特把装着山顶巢中珍贵标本的药丸盒放在树脚下，开始往上爬。


这注定是他惊奇不断的一天。爬到半途，他发现自己踩在一种平台上，似乎是树分作两股的枢纽。左边是那个巢，高居叉上。右边是另一根新枝。他立刻明白再往上也是徒劳，不禁大为扫兴。枝条显然承不住他的体重。他低头准备下去，却惊愕地发现自己正望进一个黑洞洞的穴。先前一心扑在鸟巢上，竟没注意到这树是空心的。


这下把许多缺憾都补回来了。他的扫兴淡了下去。世上比空心树更有趣的东西不多。


“不知道下面有多深，”他自言自语。他掰下一截木头丢进空腔。似乎眨眼就到了底。他又试了一截。落地声几乎同时传上来。显然洞穴不深。他双手扒住洞沿，把自己轻轻放入黑暗。双脚几乎立刻触到实物。凭判断，洞深不过五英尺。他站直身子，下巴刚好能搁在洞沿上。


===Original===

He seemed to be standing on some sort of a floor, roughly made, but too regular to be the work of nature. Evidently someone had been here before. He bent down to make certain. There was more room to move about in than he suspected. A man sitting down would find it not uncomfortable.


He brushed his hand along the floor. Certainly it seemed to be constructed of boards. Then his hand hit something small and hard. He groped about until his fingers closed on it. It was﻿—what was it? He could hardly make out for the moment. Suddenly, as he moved it, something inside it rattled. Now he knew what it was. It was the very thing he most needed, a box of matches.


The first match he struck promptly and naturally went out. No first match ever stays alight for more than three-fifths of a second. The second was more successful. The sudden light dazzled him for a moment. When his eyes had grown accustomed to it, the match went out. He lit a third, and this time he saw all round the little chamber.


“Great Scott,” he said, “the place is a regular poultry shop.”


All round the sides were hung pheasants and partridges in various stages of maturity. Here and there the fur of a rabbit or a hare showed up amongst the feathers. Barrett hit on the solution of the problem directly. He had been shown a similar collection once in a tree on his father’s land. The place was the headquarters of some poacher. Barrett was full of admiration for the ingenuity of the man in finding so safe a hiding place.


===Chinese===

他脚下似乎踩着某种地板，做工粗糙，却规整得绝非天工。显然先前有人来过。他弯腰细察。里头回旋的余地比他料想的大。一个人坐进去不会觉得难受。


他沿地面扫了扫手。确实像是木板拼的。随后手碰到一件小小的硬物。他四处摸索，直到手指扣住它。这是——这是什么？一时竟辨认不出。忽然，他一动它，里面的什么东西哗啦作响。这下他知道是什么了。正是他最需要的东西：一盒火柴。


他划的第一根火柴顺理成章地熄了。头一根火柴从来没有燃烧超过五分之三秒的。第二根运气好些。骤亮的光晃了他一下眼。等眼睛适应了，火柴又灭了。他点上第三根，这一回，他把小室四壁看了个遍。


“我的天，”他说，“这地方活脱脱一家野味铺。”


四壁挂满了生熟不一的雉鸡和鹧鸪。羽毛之间，处处露出兔子或野兔的皮毛。巴雷特立刻猜破了谜底。他曾在他父亲的地产上一棵树里见过类似的收藏。这地方是某个偷猎者的总部。巴雷特满心叹服此人竟能找到如此稳妥的藏匿之所，足见其巧思。


===Original===

He continued his search. In one angle of the tree was a piece of sacking. Barrett lifted it. He caught a glimpse of something bright, but before he could confirm the vague suspicion that flashed upon him, his match burnt down and lay smouldering on the floor. His hand trembled with excitement as he started to light another. It broke off in his hand. At last he succeeded. The light flashed up, and there beside the piece of sacking which had covered them were two cups. He recognised them instantly.


“Jove,” he gasped. “The Sports pots! Now, how on earth﻿—”


At this moment something happened which took his attention away from his discovery with painful suddenness. From beneath him came the muffled whine of a dog. He listened, holding his breath. No, he was not mistaken. The dog whined again, and broke into an excited bark. Somebody at the foot of the tree began to speak.


===Chinese===

他继续搜查。树的一个角落里有块麻布。巴雷特把它掀开。一道亮光晃入眼帘，可还没等他证实心头闪过的隐约疑窦，火柴烧到了头，闷熄在地板上。他去点另一根时，手激动得直抖。火柴在手里折断了。他终于点着了一根。火光一闪，那块曾盖着它们的麻布旁边，赫然是两座奖杯。他一眼便认了出来。


“天哪，”他倒抽一口气。“运动会的奖杯！这下，到底怎么会——”


就在此时，一件事发生了，以一种令人心惊的突然夺走了他对这项发现的注意。脚下传来一声闷闷的狗呜咽。他屏息细听。没错，他没听错。狗又呜咽一声，随即爆发出一阵狂吠。树脚下有人开口说话了。

## VIII / 八

===Original===

Barrett Ceases to Explore


“Fetchimout!” said the voice, all in one word.


“Nice cheery remark to make!” thought Barrett. “He’ll have to do a good bit of digging before he fetches *me* out. I’m a fixture for the present.”


There was a sound of scratching as if the dog, in his eagerness to oblige, were trying to uproot the tree. Barrett, realising that unless the keeper took it into his head to climb, which was unlikely, he was as safe as if he had been in his study at Philpott’s, chuckled within himself, and listened intently.


“What is it, then?” said the keeper. “Good dog, at ’em! Fetch him out, Jack.”


===Chinese===

巴雷特中止探索


“把他弄出来！”那声音一口气说道，全并成了一个词。


“这话讲得可真提气！”巴雷特心想。“想把我*弄*出去，他可有得挖了。眼下我就是钉在这儿的固定物件。”


一阵刨土声传来，仿佛那条狗出于报效的热忱，正试图把整棵树连根拔起。巴雷特意识到，除非看守一时兴起爬上来——这不太可能——否则他稳如坐在菲尔波特社自己的书房里，不由得暗自发笑，凝神细听。


“怎么回事？”看守说。“好狗子，上！把他弄出来，杰克（Jack）。”


===Original===

Jack barked excitedly, and redoubled his efforts.


The sound of scratching proceeded.


“R-r-r-ats-s-s!” said the mendacious keeper. Jack had evidently paused for breath. Barrett began quite to sympathise with him. The thought that the animal was getting farther away from the object of his search with every ounce of earth he removed, tickled him hugely. He would have liked to have been able to see the operations, though. At present it was like listening to a conversation through a telephone. He could only guess at what was going on.


Then he heard somebody whistling “The Lincolnshire Poacher,” a strangely inappropriate air in the mouth of a keeper. The sound was too far away to be the work of Jack’s owner, unless he had gone for a stroll since his last remark. No, it was another keeper. A new voice came up to him.


“ ’Ullo, Ned, what’s the dog after?”


===Chinese===

杰克兴奋地吠着，干劲倍增。


刨土声继续着。


“老——老——耗——子——子！”那撒谎成性的看守说道。杰克显然停下来喘气了。巴雷特倒相当同情起它来。一想到这畜生每刨出一把土，便离它搜寻的目标更远一分，他就乐不可支。不过他倒真想亲眼看看这场作业。眼下这就像隔着电话听人交谈，只能凭空猜测进展。


接着他听见有人吹《林肯郡偷猎者》（The Lincolnshire Poacher）——从看守嘴里吹出来，这曲子未免太不般配。声音太远，不会是杰克主人的手笔，除非他自上回发话后遛了个弯。不，是另一个看守。一个新的声音传到他耳中。


“哟，内德（Ned），狗儿追什么呢？”


===Original===

“Thinks ’e’s smelt a rabbit, seems to me.”


“Ain’t a rabbit hole ’ere.”


“Thinks there is, anyhow. Look at the pore beast!”


They both laughed. Jack meanwhile, unaware that he was turning himself into an exhibition to make a keeper’s holiday, dug assiduously. “Come away, Jack,” said the first keeper at length. “Ain’t nothin’ there. Ought to know that, clever dog like you.”


There was a sound as if he had pulled Jack bodily from his hole.


===Chinese===

“我瞧它是闻着兔子味儿了。”


“这儿可没有兔子窝。”


“不管咋说，它认定有。瞧这可怜的畜生！”


两人大笑。杰克浑然不知自己正在沦为给看守假日助兴的展览，兀自勤勤恳恳地刨。“过来，杰克，”头一个看守终于说，“那儿啥也没有。像你这么聪明的狗，该明白这个。”


一阵声响，仿佛他把杰克整个儿从洞里拽了出来。


===Original===

“Wait! ’Ere, Ned, what’s that on the ground there?” Barrett gasped. His pillboxes had been discovered. Surely they would put two and two together now, and climb the tree after him.


“Eggs. Two of ’em. ’Ow did they get ’ere, then?”


“It’s one of them young devils from the School. Master says to me this morning, ‘Look out,’ ’e says, ‘Saunders, for them boys as come in ’ere after eggs, and frighten all the birds out of the dratted place. You keep your eyes open, Saunders,’ ’e says.”


“Well, if ’e’s still in the woods, we’ll ’ave ’im safe.”


“*If* he’s still in the woods!” thought Barrett with a shiver.


===Chinese===

“慢着！喏，内德，地上那是啥？”巴雷特倒抽一口气。他的药丸盒被发现了。这下他们必然会前后一串联，跟着爬上树来抓他了。


“蛋。两盒。咋会到这儿的呢？”


“准是学校那帮小魔鬼里的一个。今儿早上老爷跟我说：‘当心点，’他说，‘桑德斯（Saunders），留神那些进来掏蛋、把鸟儿从这鬼地方吓跑的学生。你给我睁大眼睛，桑德斯，’他说。”


“哼，他要还在林子里，咱们稳稳拿住他。”


“但愿他还‘在林子里’！”巴雷特打了个寒噤想道。


===Original===

After this there was silence. Barrett waited for what he thought was a quarter of an hour﻿—it was really five minutes or less﻿—then he peeped cautiously over the edge of his hiding place. Yes, they had certainly gone, unless﻿—horrible thought﻿—they were waiting so close to the trunk of the tree as to be invisible from where he stood. He decided that the possibility must be risked. He was down on the ground in record time. Nothing happened. No hand shot out from its ambush to clutch him. He breathed more freely, and began to debate within himself which way to go. Up the hill it must be, of course, but should he go straight up, or to the left or to the right? He would have given much to know which way the keepers had gone, particularly he of the dog. They had separated, he knew. He began to reason the thing out. In the first place if they had separated, they must have gone different ways. It did not take him long to arrive at that conclusion. The odds, therefore, were that one had gone to the right upstream, the other downstream to the left. His knowledge of human nature told him that nobody would willingly walk uphill if it was possible for him to walk on the flat. Therefore, assuming the two keepers to be human, they had gone along the valley. Therefore, his best plan would be to make straight for the top of the hill, as straight as he could steer, and risk it. Just as he was about to start, his eye caught the two pillboxes, lying on the turf a few yards from where he had placed them.


“May as well take what I can get,” he thought. He placed them carefully in his pocket. As he did so a faint bark came to him on the breeze from downstream. That must be friend Jack. He waited no longer, but dived into the bushes in the direction of the summit. He was congratulating himself on being out of danger﻿—already he was more than halfway up the hill﻿—when suddenly he received a terrible shock. From the bushes to his left, not ten yards from where he stood, came the clear, sharp sound of a whistle. The sound was repeated, and this time an answer came from far out to his right. Before he could move another whistle joined in, again from the left, but farther off and higher up the hill than the first he had heard. He recalled what Grey had said about “millions” of keepers. The expression, he thought, had understated the true facts, if anything. He remembered the case of Babington. It was a moment for action. No guile could save him now. It must be a stern chase for the rest of the distance. He drew a breath, and was off like an arrow. The noise he made was appalling. No one in the wood could help hearing it.


“Stop, there!” shouted someone. The voice came from behind, a fact which he noted almost automatically and rejoiced at. He had a start at any rate.


“Stop!” shouted the voice once again. The whistle blew like a steam siren, and once more the other two answered it. They were all behind him now. Surely a man of the public schools in flannels and gymnasium shoes, and trained to the last ounce for just such a sprint as this, could beat a handful of keepers in their leggings and heavy boots. Barrett raced on. Close behind him a crashing in the undergrowth and the sound of heavy breathing told him that keeper number one was doing his best. To left and right similar sounds were to be heard. But Barrett had placed these competitors out of the running at once. The race was between him and the man behind.


Fifty yards of difficult country, bushes which caught his clothes as if they were trying to stop him in the interests of law and order, branches which lashed him across the face, and rabbit holes half hidden in the bracken, and still he kept his lead. He was increasing it. He must win now. The man behind was panting in deep gasps, for the pace had been warm and he was not in training. Barrett cast a glance over his shoulder, and as he looked the keeper’s foot caught in a hole and he fell heavily. Barrett uttered a shout of triumph. Victory was his.


===Chinese===

此后是一片沉寂。巴雷特等了他以为的一刻钟——其实不过五分钟或更短——然后小心翼翼地从藏身处的边沿探头张望。是的，他们肯定走了，除非——这念头很可怕——他们正埋伏在紧贴树干的地方，从他站的位置望不见。他断定这个险必须冒。他破纪录地一转眼便落到了地面。什么也没发生。没有一只手从埋伏中窜出抓他。他呼吸畅快了些，开始在心中盘算走哪条路。当然必须上山，可是直上，还是靠左，靠右？若能知道看守们往哪边去了——尤其牵狗那位——他出什么代价都愿意。他们分开了，这他知道。他开始推理。首先，既然分开，必然走了不同的路。这个结论没费他多少工夫。因此，多半是一个往右去了上游，一个往左去了下游。他对人性的了解告诉他：能走平路的人，谁也不肯自愿爬山。因此，姑且假定这两位看守是人类，他们便是沿谷底走了。因此，他的上策便是笔直取山顶，能瞄多直瞄多直，豁出去了。正要动身，他的目光瞥见了那两盒药丸盒，躺在离他原先放置处几码远的草皮上。


“能捞多少捞多少吧，”他想。他把盒子小心放进衣袋。就在这时，下游方向顺着风飘来一声隐约的犬吠。准是杰克那位朋友。他不再耽搁，一头扎进朝向山顶的灌木丛。他正自庆幸脱离险境——已爬过半山——猛然间遭到重重一吓。左边的灌木丛里，距他站立处不足十码，响起一声清脆尖利的哨音。哨音再响，这一回，远远的右方传来了应和。他还来不及动，第三声哨子又加入进来，仍在左边，却比头一声更远、更高。他想起格雷关于“上万”看守的话。这说法，他思忖，若说有什么不妥，那也是说轻了。他记起了巴宾顿的前车之鉴。此刻唯有行动。什么计谋都救不了他了。余下的路程只能硬碰硬地追逃。他吸一口气，箭一般窜了出去。他弄出的动静骇人听闻。林子里没人能听不见。


“站住！”有人大喝。声音来自身后——他几乎是下意识地记下这一点，并为之窃喜。好歹他抢了先手。


“站住！”那声音再次大喝。哨子吹得像汽笛，另外两支又应和起来。这会儿全在他身后了。一个公学出身、身穿法兰绒裤脚蹬体操鞋、为的正是这种冲刺而训练到最后一分力气的人，总该跑得过几个裹着绑腿、蹬着笨重靴子的看守。巴雷特继续飞奔。身后紧处，下木的噼啪声和粗重的喘息声告诉他，一号看守已尽了全力。左右两侧也传来类似的声响。但巴雷特早把这两位竞争者甩出了局。真正的较量在他与身后那人之间。


五十码艰难地形：灌木钩扯他的衣裳，仿佛出于维护法纪的考虑要拦下他；枝条抽打他的脸；兔子洞半掩在蕨草间——而他依然领先。而且还在扩大。这下他赢定了。身后那人喘得深沉，毕竟速度已然火热，他又缺乏训练。巴雷特回头瞟了一眼，正看着，看守的脚陷进一个洞里，重重摔倒。巴雷特发出一声胜利的呐喊。胜利是他的了。


===Original===

In front of him was a small hollow fringed with bushes. Collecting his strength he cleared these with a bound. Then another of the events of this eventful afternoon happened. Instead of the hard turf, his foot struck something soft, something which sat up suddenly with a yell. Barrett rolled down the slope and halfway up the other side like a shot rabbit. Dimly he recognised that he had jumped on to a human being. The figure did not wear the official velveteens. Therefore he had no business in the Dingle. And close behind thundered the keeper, now on his feet once more, dust on his clothes and wrath in his heart in equal proportions. “Look out, man!” shouted Barrett, as the injured person rose to his feet. “Run! Cut, quick! Keeper!” There was no time to say more. He ran. Another second and he was at the top, over the railing, and in the good, honest, public high road again, safe. A hoarse shout of “Got yer!” from below told a harrowing tale of capture. The stranger had fallen into the hands of the enemy. Very cautiously Barrett left the road and crept to the railing again. It was a rash thing to do, but curiosity overcame him. He had to see, or, if that was impossible, to hear what had happened.


For a moment the only sound to be heard was the gasping of the keeper. After a few seconds a rapidly nearing series of crashes announced the arrival of the man from the right flank of the pursuing forces, while almost simultaneously his colleague on the left came up.


Barrett could see nothing, but it was easy to understand what was going on. Keeper number one was exhibiting his prisoner. His narrative, punctuated with gasps, was told mostly in hoarse whispers, and Barrett missed most of it.


“Foot (gasp) rabbit ’ole.” More gasps. “Up agen﻿ ﻿… minute﻿ ﻿… (indistinct mutterings)﻿ ﻿… and (triumphantly) **cotched ’im**!”


Exclamations of approval from the other two. “I assure you,” said another voice. The prisoner was having his say. “I assure you that I was doing no harm whatever in this wood. I.﻿ ﻿…”


===Chinese===

前方是一小片洼地，四周灌木环生。他凝聚余力，一跃而过。随后，这个多事下午的又一桩事件发生了。落脚处不是坚硬草皮，而是某种软乎乎的东西——那东西尖叫一声霍然坐起。巴雷特像只中了弹的兔子，滚下坡又滚上对面半坡。他迷迷糊糊地意识到，自己跳到了一个大活人身上。那身影穿的并非看守的制式天鹅绒服。因此他无权待在丁格尔。而看守紧随其后轰隆而至，此时已重新爬起，衣上的尘土与心中的怒火旗鼓相当。“小心，老兄！”那人起身时巴雷特大喊，“快跑！快撤！看守！”来不及多说。他跑了。再一秒钟，他已登顶，翻过栏杆，重回那条善良诚实的公路，安全了。下面传来一声嘶哑的“逮着你了！”，讲述了一段令人揪心的被俘始末。那陌生人落入敌手了。巴雷特小心翼翼地离开大路，又匍匐回栏杆边。这是鲁莽之举，但好奇心占了上风。他非得看看——实在看不成，也要听听发生了什么。


有那么一刻，唯一听得见的声音是看守的喘息。几秒钟后，一串急近的踩踏声宣告追捕部队右翼那位赶到，与此同时，左翼的同事也几乎同时到达。


巴雷特什么也看不见，但不难明白正在发生什么。一号看守正在展示他的俘虏。他的叙述以喘息为标点，多用嘶哑的耳语讲出，巴雷特大半没听清。


“脚（喘）兔子窝。”又是几喘。“撞上……一下……（含糊的咕哝）……然后（得意洋洋地）**把他逮住了**！”


另外两人发出赞许的惊叹。“我向你保证，”另一个声音说道。俘虏开始发言。“我保证我在这林子里绝无半点害处行为。我……”


===Original===

“Better tell that tale to Sir Alfred,” cut in one of his captors.


“ ’E’ll learn yer,” said the keeper previously referred to as number one, vindictively. He was feeling shaken up with his run and his heavy fall, and his temper was proportionately short.


“I swear I’ve heard that voice before somewhere,” thought Barrett. “Wonder if it’s a Coll. chap.”


Keeper number one added something here, which was inaudible to Barrett.


“I tell you I’m not a poacher,” said the prisoner, indignantly. “And I object to your language. I tell you I was lying here doing nothing and some fool or other came and jumped on me. I.﻿ ﻿…”


===Chinese===

“这故事留着讲给阿尔弗雷德爵士听吧，”捕他的人之一插嘴道。


“他会好好教教你，”前文称为一号的看守恶狠狠地说。他跑了这一场又摔了重重一跤，正觉得五脏六腑都移了位，火气也就相应地短不了。


“我发誓这嗓音从前在哪儿听过，”巴雷特想道，“不知是不是公学里的人。”


一号看守此时又补充了几句什么，巴雷特听不见。


“我说了我不是偷猎的，”俘虏愤慨地说，“我还嫌你们的用词难听。我告诉你，我正躺在这儿什么也没干，不知哪个蠢货跑来一屁股跳在我身上。我……”


===Original===

The rest was inaudible. But Barrett had heard enough.


“I knew I’d heard that voice before. Plunkett, by Jove! Golly, what is the world coming to, when heads of Houses and School prefects go on the poach! Fancy! Plunkett of all people, too! This is a knockout, I’m hanged if it isn’t.”


From below came the sound of movement. The keepers were going down the hill again. To Barrett’s guilty conscience it seemed that they were coming up. He turned and fled.


The hedge separating Sir Alfred Venner’s land from the road was not a high one, though the drop the other side was considerable. Barrett had not reckoned on this. He leapt the hedge, and staggered across the road. At the same moment a grey-clad cyclist, who was pedalling in a leisurely manner in the direction of the School, arrived at the spot. A collision seemed imminent, but the stranger in a perfectly composed manner, as if he had suddenly made up his mind to take a sharp turning, rode his machine up the bank, whence he fell with easy grace to the road, just in time to act as a cushion for Barrett. The two lay there in a tangled heap. Barrett was the first to rise.


===Chinese===

后面的话听不见了。但巴雷特听得已经够多。


“我就说听过这嗓音。普伦基特，老天作证！乖乖，这世道要到什么地步，社长和校级级长都干起偷猎来了！想想吧！偏偏还是普伦基特！这一下可真是击倒了，要不是才怪。”


下面传来动静。看守们又下山去了。可在巴雷特问心有愧的耳朵里，那仿佛是在上来。他转身便逃。


隔开阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士地产与大路的树篱不算高，虽然另一侧的落差相当可观。巴雷特没料到这一层。他跃过树篱，踉跄着横穿大路。同一时刻，一位灰衣骑车人正朝学校方向悠然蹬来，恰好到达此处。相撞眼看难免，可那位陌生人以十足从容的姿态——仿佛突然决意来个急转弯——把车骑上路堤，随即又以潇洒的优雅姿态跌回路面，不早不晚，正好给巴雷特当了肉垫。两人乱作一堆躺在那里。巴雷特先爬了起来。

## IX / 九

===Original===

Enter the Sleuthhound


“I’m awfully sorry,” he said, disentangling himself carefully from the heap. “I hope you’re not hurt.”


The man did not reply for a moment. He appeared to be laying the question before himself as an impartial judge, as who should say: “Now tell me candidly, *are* you hurt? Speak freely and without bias.”


“No,” he said at last, feeling his left leg as if he were not absolutely easy in his mind about that, “no, not hurt, thank you. Not much, that is,” he added with the air of one who thinks it best to qualify too positive a statement. “Left leg. Shin. Slight bruise. Nothing to signify.”


“It was a rotten thing to do, jumping over into the road like that,” said Barrett. “Didn’t remember there’d be such a big drop.”


===Chinese===

神探登场


“实在对不住，”他一面小心翼翼地从人堆里抽身，一面说，“但愿您没受伤。”


那人一时没有作答。他似乎正把这个问题提交给自己这位公正的法官，仿佛在说：“现在坦白告诉我，你*到底*伤了没有？尽管直言，勿存偏袒。”


“没有，”他终于说道，一边摸着左腿，仿佛对此尚未全然放心，“没伤，谢谢。或者说，伤得不重，”他补了一句，一副认为最好给过于肯定的说法打个折扣的神气。“左腿。胫骨。轻微淤伤。无关紧要。”


“就这么往路里跳，实在太混账了，”巴雷特说，“没想起来落差会有这么大。”


===Original===

“My fault in a way,” said the man. “Riding wrong side of road. Out for a run?”


“More or less.”


“Excellent thing.”


“Yes.”


It occurred to Barrett that it was only due to the man on whom he had been rolling to tell him the true facts of the case. Besides, it might do something towards removing the impression which must, he felt, be forming in the stranger’s mind that he was mad.


===Chinese===

“多多少少也算我的不是，”那人说，“我靠错了路侧。出来练跑的？”


“差不多。”


“好事情。”


“是啊。”


巴雷特想到，自己刚才压着人家滚了半天，把实情相告才算对得起他。再说，他察觉这位陌生人心目中正形成一个“他是疯子”的印象，实话或许能起点消解作用。


===Original===

“You see,” he said, in a burst of confidence, “it was rather a close thing. There were some keepers after me.”


“Ah!” said the man. “Thought so. Trespassing?”


“Yes.”


“Ah. Keepers don’t like trespassers. Curious thing﻿—don’t know if it ever occurred to you﻿—if there were no trespassers, there would be no need for keepers. To their interest, then, to encourage trespassers. But do they?”


Barrett admitted that they did not very conspicuously.


===Chinese===

“是这样的，”他一时冲动，把底细和盘托出，“方才相当惊险。有几个看守在追我。”


“啊！”那人说，“我就料到。擅闯私地？”


“是。”


“啊。看守不喜欢擅闯者。说来奇怪——不知你可曾想到过——要是没有擅闯者，就用不着看守。如此说来，鼓励擅闯者才符合他们的利益。可他们干吗？”


巴雷特承认，他们干得并不怎么起劲。


===Original===

“No. Same with all professions. Not poaching, I suppose?”


“Rather not. I was after eggs. By Jove, that reminds me.” He felt in his pocket for the pillboxes. Could they have survived the stormy times through which they had been passing? He heaved a sigh of relief as he saw that the eggs were uninjured. He was so intent on examining them that he missed the stranger’s next remark.


“Sorry. What? I didn’t hear.”


“Asked if I was going right for St. Austin’s School.”


“College!” said Barrett with a convulsive shudder. The most deadly error mortal man can make, with the exception of calling a school a college, is to call a college a school.


===Chinese===

“就是。各行皆然。不是去偷猎的吧，我猜？”


“当然不是。我是去掏蛋的。天哪，这倒提醒了我。”他往口袋里摸药丸盒。它们可从这一路惊涛骇浪里幸存下来了？看到蛋完好无损，他如释重负地长出一口气。他专心检视蛋件，把陌生人接下来说的话漏了过去。


“抱歉。什么？我没听见。”


“我问去圣奥斯汀学校（School）这条路对不对。”


“是学院（College）！”巴雷特说着，猛打一个寒噤。凡人所能犯下的最致命错误——把学校唤作学院除外——便是把学院唤作学校。


===Original===

“College!” said the man. “Is this the road?”


“Yes. You can’t miss it. I’m going there myself. It’s only about a mile.”


“Ah,” said the man, with a touch of satisfaction in his voice. “Going there yourself, are you? Perhaps you’re one of the scholars?”


“Not much,” said Barrett, “ask our form beak if I’m a scholar. Oh. I see. Yes, I’m there all right.”


Barrett was a little puzzled as to how to class his companion. No old public school man would talk of scholars. And yet he was emphatically not a bargee. Barrett set him down as a sort of superior tourist, a Henry as opposed to an ’Arry.


===Chinese===

“学院！”那人说，“是走这条路？”


“对。一走就到，错不了。我自己也去那儿。也就一英里上下。”


“啊，”那人说，声音里带着一丝满意，“自己也去，是吗？莫非你是那儿的读书种子（scholar）之一？”


“差得远，”巴雷特说，“配不配读书种子，去问我们班的老师。哦，我懂了。是，我确实是那儿的学生。”


该怎么把这位同伴归类，巴雷特有点犯难。老公学出身的人绝不会谈什么读书种子。可他又断乎不是个驳船夫。巴雷特把他归为某种高等游客——是“亨利”（Henry），而非“阿利”（’Arry）。


===Original===

“Been bit of a disturbance there, hasn’t there? Cricket pavilion. Cups.”


“Rather. But how on earth﻿—”


“How on earth did I get to hear of it, you were going to say. Well, no need to conceal anything. Fact is, down here to look into the matter. Detective. Name, Roberts, Scotland Yard. Now we know each other, and if you can tell me one or two things about this burglary, it would be a great help to me, and I should be very much obliged.”


Barrett had heard that a detective was coming down to look into the affair of the cups. His position was rather a difficult one. In a sense it was simple enough. He had found the cups. He could (keepers permitting) go and fetch them now, and there would﻿—No. There would *not* be an end of the matter. It would be very pleasant, exceedingly pleasant, to go to the Headmaster and the detective, and present the cups to them with a “Bless you, my children” air. The Headmaster would say, “Barrett, you’re a marvel. How can I thank you sufficiently?” while the detective would observe that he had been in the profession over twenty years, but never had he seen so remarkable an exhibition of sagacity and acumen as this. That, at least, was what ought to take place. But Barrett’s experience of life, short as it was, had taught him the difference between the ideal and the real. The real, he suspected, would in this case be painful. Certain facts would come to light. When had he found the cups? About four in the afternoon? Oh. Roll-call took place at four in the afternoon. How came it that he was not at roll-call? Furthermore, how came it that he was marked on the list as having answered his name at that ceremony? Where had he found the cups? In a hollow tree? Just so. Where was the hollow tree? In Sir Alfred Venner’s woods. Did he know that Sir Alfred Venner’s woods were out of bounds? Did he know that, in consequence of complaints from Sir Alfred Venner, Sir Alfred Venner’s woods were more out of bounds than any other out of bounds woods in the entire county that did *not* belong to Sir Alfred Venner? He did? Ah! No, the word for his guidance in this emergency, he felt instinctively, was “mum.” Time might provide him with a solution. He might, for instance, abstract the cups secretly from their resting place, place them in the middle of the football field, and find them there dramatically after morning school. Or he might reveal his secret from the carriage window as his train moved out of the station on the first day of the holidays. There was certain to be some way out of the difficulty. But for the present, silence.


He answered his companion’s questions freely, however. Of the actual burglary he knew no more than any other member of the School, considerably less, indeed, than Jim Thomson, of Merevale’s, at present staggering under the weight of a secret even more gigantic than Barrett’s own. In return for his information he extracted sundry reminiscences. The scar on the detective’s cheekbone, barely visible now, was the mark of a bullet, which a certain burglar, named, singularly enough, Roberts, had fired at him from a distance of five yards. The gentleman in question, who, the detective hastened to inform Barrett, was no relation of his, though owning the same name, happened to be a poor marksman and only scored a bad outer, assuming the detective’s face to have been the bull. He also turned up his cuff to show a larger scar. This was another testimonial from the burglar world. A Kensington practitioner had had the bad taste to bite off a piece of that part of the detective. In short, Barrett enlarged his knowledge of the seamy side of things considerably in the mile of road which had to be traversed before St. Austin’s appeared in sight. The two parted at the big gates, Barrett going in the direction of Philpott’s, the detective wheeling his machine towards the porter’s lodge.


===Chinese===

“那儿出了点乱子，是吧？板球休息亭。奖杯。”


“可不是。可您到底是怎么——”


“您是想说，我到底是怎么听说的。呃，无须隐瞒什么。实情是，我为此事下来查办的。侦探。贱名罗伯茨（Roberts），苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）。这下咱们互相认识了。你要是能就这桩盗窃案告诉我一两件事，对我帮助极大，我将感激不尽。”


巴雷特听说有侦探要下来查奖杯的案子。他的处境颇为微妙。某种意义上也简单：他找到了奖杯。只要（看守们允许）现在就去取来，事情就——不。事情*不会*就此了结。要是能去见校长和侦探，把奖杯往他们跟前一放，摆出一副“孩子们，不用谢”的气派，那自然妙极，妙不可言。校长会说：“巴雷特，你是个奇才。叫我如何谢你才好？”侦探则会表示，自己干这行二十多年，从未见过如此非凡的睿智与敏锐。至少，理当如此上演。但巴雷特的人生阅历尽管尚浅，已教会他理想与现实的分别。他疑心这一回的现实会很不好受。某些事实将浮出水面。他何时找到的奖杯？下午四点上下？哦。下午四点正在点名。他怎么会不在点名现场？再者，名册上怎么把他记成在该仪式上应过到？他在哪儿找到的奖杯？一棵空心树里？正是。空心树在哪儿？阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士的林子里。他可知道阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士的林子在界外？他可知道，由于阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士的投诉，在全郡所有不属于阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士的界外林子当中，阿尔弗雷德·文纳爵士的林子比哪一片都更加界外？他知道？啊！不，他凭本能意识到，此刻指引他的箴言是“缄默”。时间自会给他一条出路。比如，他可以悄悄把奖杯从藏匿处取走，放到足球场正中央，待上午课后戏剧性地“发现”它们。或者等到放假第一天，火车驶出站台时，他从车窗里吐露自己的秘密。困难必有一条出路。但目前，沉默。


不过，他回答同伴的问题倒毫无保留。至于盗窃本身，他不比学校其他人多知道什么——其实比梅雷维尔社的吉姆·汤姆森少得多，那一位此刻正背负着一桩比巴雷特的还重大的秘密蹒跚而行。作为情报交换，他套出了对方的若干掌故。侦探颧骨上那道如今已很淡的疤，是一枚子弹的纪念——某个出奇地也叫罗伯茨的窃贼，在五码开外朝他开的。那位先生——侦探连忙向巴雷特声明，虽与他同姓，却非其亲故——碰巧枪法蹩脚，只命中一个坏外环（假定侦探的脸是靶心的话）。他还翻起袖口，亮出一道更大的疤。这是窃贼界的又一封荐书。肯辛顿（Kensington）一位从业者曾口味欠佳，咬下过侦探那个部位的一块肉。总之，在望见圣奥斯汀之前那一英里路上，巴雷特大大充实了自己对世间阴暗面的认识。两人在大门口分了手，巴雷特往菲尔波特社去，侦探推着车往门房去。


===Original===

Barrett’s condition when he turned in at Philpott’s door was critical. He was so inflated with news that any attempt to keep it in might have serious results. Certainly he could not sleep that night in such a bomb-like state.


It was thus that he broke in upon Reade. Reade had passed an absurdly useless afternoon. He had not stirred from the study. For all that it would have mattered to him, it might have been raining hard the whole afternoon, instead of being, as it had been, the finest afternoon of the whole term. In a word, and not to put too fine a point on the matter, he had been frousting, and consequently was feeling dull and sleepy, and generally under-vitalised and futile. Barrett entered the study with a rush, and was carried away by excitement to such an extent that he addressed Reade as if the deadly feud between them not only did not exist, but never had existed.


“I say, Reade. Heave that beastly book away. My aunt, I have had an afternoon of it.”


“Oh?” said Reade, politely, “where did you go?”


“After eggs in the Dingle.”


===Chinese===

巴雷特跨进菲尔波特社大门时，处境危殆。他被满肚子的新闻撑得饱饱的，任何憋住不吐的企图都可能导致严重后果。带着这样一颗炸弹的状态，那晚他肯定睡不着。


他就是这样闯到里德面前的。里德刚过了一个荒唐而无用的下午。他没挪出书房半步。于他而言，纵然整个下午大雨滂沱也无关痛痒——而实际上，那是全学期最美好的一个下午。一句话，直截了当地说：他焖了一下午，因而正觉昏沉困倦，整个人元气不足、百无一用。巴雷特一阵风冲进书房，兴奋得忘乎所以，竟同里德攀谈起来，仿佛两人之间那场殊死纷争不但不存在，而且从未存在过。


“我说，里德。把那破书扔了吧。我的老天，我这个下午可够受的。”


“哦？”里德客气地说，“去哪儿了？”


“去丁格尔掏蛋了。”


===Original===

Reade was fairly startled out of his dignified reserve. For the first time since they had had their little difference, he addressed Barrett in a sensible manner.


“You idiot!” he said.


“Don’t see it. The Dingle’s just the place to spend a happy day. Like Rosherville. Jove, it’s worth going there. You should see the birds. Place is black with ’em.”


“How about keepers? See any?”


“Did I not! Three of them chased me like good ’uns all over the place.”


===Chinese===

里德着实从矜持的城府里惊跳出来。自两人闹了那点不快以来，他头一回用正常的口气同巴雷特说话。


“你这个白痴！”他说。


“我看挺好。丁格尔正是消磨快活日子的好去处。跟罗什维尔（Rosherville）似的。天哪，那地方值得一去。你真该瞧瞧那些鸟。满地方都是，黑压压一片。”


“看守呢？见着了吗？”


“何止见着！三个看守满地方追我，一个个敬业得很。”


===Original===

“You got away all right, though.”


“Only just. I say, do you know what happened? You know that rotter Plunkett. Used to be a day boy. Head of Ward’s now. Wears specs.”


“Yes?”


“Well, just as I was almost out of the wood, I jumped a bush and landed right on top of him. The man was asleep or something. Fancy choosing the Dingle of all places to sleep in, where you can’t go a couple of yards without running into a keeper! He hadn’t even the sense to run. I yelled to him to look out, and then I hooked it myself. And then the nearest keeper, who’d just come down a buster over a rabbit hole, sailed in and had him. I couldn’t do anything, of course.”


“Jove, there’ll be a fair-sized row about this. The Old Man’s on to trespassing like tar. I say, think Plunkett’ll say anything about you being there too?”


===Chinese===

“不过你总算脱身了。”


“勉强算是。我说，你知道出了什么事吗？你认识那个讨厌鬼普伦基特。以前是走读生。现在是沃德社社长。戴眼镜的那个。”


“然后？”


“呃，就在我快出林子的时候，我跳过一丛灌木，不偏不倚落在他身上。那人正睡觉还是怎么的。想想吧，睡觉偏挑丁格尔这种地方，走不出两码就要撞上看守！他连跑的机灵劲儿都没有。我大喊叫他当心，自己就溜之大吉了。随后，刚在兔子洞上摔了个大跟头的那个看守赶到，把他逮了个正着。我当然无能为力。”


“天哪，这事要闹出不小的乱子了。老头子抓越界抓得像沥青一样粘。我说，你看普伦基特会不会把你也在场的事供出来？”


===Original===

“Shouldn’t think so. For one thing I don’t think he recognised me. Probably doesn’t know me by sight, and he was fast asleep, too. No, I fancy I’m all right.”


“Well, it was a jolly narrow shave. Anything else happen?”


“Anything else! Just a bit. That’s to say, no, nothing much else. No.”


“Now then,” said Reade, briskly. “None of your beastly mysteries. Out with it.”


“Look here, swear you’ll keep it dark?”


===Chinese===

“我看不会。一来我不觉得他认出我了。多半连见都没见过我，况且他还睡得死沉。不，我估摸自己没事。”


“总之，真是千钧一发。还发生别的了吗？”


“还发生别的！可不老少。呃，就是说，没了，没别的什么大事。没了。”


“行了，”里德干脆地说，“别搞你那套讨厌的卖关子。招吧。”


“听着，发誓保密？”


===Original===

“Of course I will.”


“On your word of honour?”


“If you think﻿—” began Reade in an offended voice.


“No, it’s all right. Don’t get shirty. The thing is, though, it’s so frightfully important to keep it dark.”


“Well? Buck up.”


===Chinese===

“当然保密。”


“以名誉起誓？”


“你要是觉得——”里德委屈地开了腔。


“别，没事。别动气。只是，这事保起密来天大地大，非同小可。”


“然后呢？快点。”


===Original===

“Well, you needn’t believe me, of course, but I’ve found the pots.”


Reade gasped.


“What!” he cried. “The pot for the quarter?”


“And the one for the hundred yards. Both of them. It’s a fact.”


“But where? How? What have you done with them?”


===Chinese===

“呃，你尽可以不信，不过——奖杯，我找到了。”


里德倒抽一口气。


“什么！”他叫道，“四分之一英里那座？”


“还有百码那座。两座都在。千真万确。”


“可在哪儿？怎么找着的？你现在把它们怎么处置的？”


===Original===

Barrett unfolded his tale concisely.


“You see,” he concluded, “what a hole I’m in. I can’t tell the Old Man anything about it, or I get booked for cutting roll-call, and going out of bounds. And then, while I’m waiting and wondering what to do, and all that, the thief, whoever he is, will most likely go off with the pots. What do you think I ought to do?”


Reade perpended.


“Well,” he said, “all you can do is to lie low and trust to luck, as far as I can see. Besides, there’s one consolation. This Plunkett business’ll make every keeper in the Dingle twice as keen after trespassers. So the pot man won’t get a chance of getting the things away.”


“Yes, there’s something in that,” admitted Barrett.


===Chinese===

巴雷特把事情经过简明扼要地摊了开来。


“你瞧，”他总结道，“我陷得多深。这事我没法向老头子吐露半个字，不然就得因旷点名加越界吃记过。可我这边还在等啊、盘算啊什么的，那贼——不管他是谁——十有八九早把奖杯卷跑了。你说我该怎么办？”


里德沉思起来。


“依我看，”他说，“你唯一能做的就是潜伏不动，听天由命。再说还有一层安慰。普伦基特这档子事，会让丁格尔的每个看守追擅闯者追得加倍起劲。所以偷杯贼再没机会把东西运走了。”
